|At CSRNG and EDN, the aligned 64-bit read carries the 32-bit register at its address in bits [31:0], as the DRBG lane adapter defines (`hw/ip/drbg/doc/architecture.adoc:69`). The 32-bit golden must be non-zero. The other lane is not defined by the spec and is not graded.
|CHK-WIDE-NONVAC
|Every aperture in the table was presented, at least three compared a non-zero value, and at least two had distinct non-zero halves. A pair that reads zero compares 0 against 0, and equal or zero halves hide some mutation classes; the two floors make the exact comparisons and the representative zero/duplicate/swap sensitivity explicit. Also requires that the slot spread ran at every aperture.
|CHK-WIDE-OUTSTANDING
|Concurrent 64-bit reads at the entropy source, distinct ids AND distinct addresses, each returning its own data and an OKAY response. The goldens must hold at least two distinct values, else a read answered with another read's data would compare equal and go unseen.
|CHK-WIDE-SLOTS
|Concurrent 64-bit reads at every aperture, distinct ids, each returning the golden. The same integrity claim as CHK-WIDE-OUTSTANDING, carried to every host path in the table rather than only the entropy source, so an aperture answering a concurrent read with zeros or stale data fails. A read answered with another read's data is not visible here because every id reads the same word; CHK-WIDE-OUTSTANDING owns that case with distinct addresses.
|===
*Evidence*: every pair has a non-zero generated reset value. CSRNG (`INT_STATE_READ_ENABLE`) and EDN (`BOOT_INS_CMD`) have a non-zero low word for the lane check. KMAC (`CFG_REGWEN` / `CFG_SHADOWED`) and the entropy source (`COMPONENT_ID` / `CTRL`) have distinct non-zero halves and carry the full zero/duplicate/swap mutation sensitivity. AES has equal non-zero halves; HMAC and OTBN have one zero half. Those three still require the 64-bit value to equal their two 32-bit reads exactly, but do not claim that every half mutation is observable on that pair. Addresses come from the generated register header by symbol, never a literal.

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope note*: the walked set is the host-path list in `hw/sys/sep/doc/crypto.adoc:66-72`; the per-path contract comes from that path's spec. The write direction is out of scope: these are status and configuration registers, and writing them to obtain a comparand would test the register rather than the 64-bit read path.

[[sep_axi_partial_lane_read_test]]
===== `sep_axi_partial_lane_read_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Fabric and remap

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_axi_partial_lane_read_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Testlist*: `system.toml` (`reseed = 3`), also in `all.toml` / `cpu_stub`

*Randomization*: RANDCFG — `SepAxiPartialLaneReadCfg` is the single source of truth for stimulus and goldens. Required cells walk every seed: all 36 contiguous (offset, length) byte-run slices of a 64-bit word (`8+7+…+1`), all 14 aligned narrow-ARSIZE cells (AxSIZE 0/1/2 at every legal lane offset: `8+4+2`), and the all-zeros / all-ones CSR patterns. The seed varies walk order, SRAM address, the 64-bit word, and extra CSR patterns only.

*Objective*: A read narrower than the 64-bit CPU-LSU beat only obligates the responder to drive the addressed byte lanes: a 32-bit CSR endpoint leaves RDATA[63:32] undriven, and a narrow-ARSIZE beat leaves every non-addressed lane undriven. The VIP master samples the full beat and extracts the addressed bytes, so the value a test receives must be a pure function of the addressed lanes — exact against an independent golden and identical on re-read — regardless of what the undriven lanes carry. `sep_axi_strobe_window_test` owns the write-side lane rule; here the write is trusted and the read extraction is on trial. The contract is pinned through the public VIP read path alone, with no cocotb or cocotbext-axi global state involved.

*Test Coverage*: Partial-lane reads on the LSU splice.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Log the seed, word, addresses and cell counts, and hold the walked counts against the 8-byte-beat floors (36 slices, 14 ARSIZE cells) plus the two required CSR extremes.
. Write a pattern to the 32-bit `SW_DEBUG` CSR, read it back twice as 4-byte reads with a checked read of the non-zero RO `SEP_LOCAL_BASE_ADDR` register between them; repeat for the all-zeros, all-ones and seed-random patterns.
. Prime a seed-random 64-bit SRAM word and its neighbour with the complement value.
. Read every contiguous (offset, length) byte run of the primed word, re-reading each slice after a checked full-width read of the complement neighbour.
. Read the primed word at every aligned narrow-ARSIZE cell (AxSIZE 0/1/2 at each legal lane offset), re-reading each cell after the same complement-neighbour disturb.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-RANDCFG
|Walked slice and ARSIZE counts equal the combinatorial floors (`36`, `14`), and the CSR walk starts with all-zeros then all-ones. Seed, word, addresses and counts are logged, so a shrunken walk fails rather than reporting PASS over fewer cells.
|CHK-CSR-LANE
|Each pattern reads back exactly and identically on the repeat, through a beat whose upper four lanes the CSR endpoint never drives. All-zeros always runs next to all-ones and random patterns on the same register, so a dead write path or a zero-filled read cannot pass the set.
|CHK-SRAM-SLICE
|Every byte-run slice equals the Python slice of the primed word, and re-reads it exactly after the disturbing read. An extraction that starts at the wrong byte, takes the wrong length, or depends on leftover channel payloads fails the compare; the disturbing read is itself compared against the complement word, so the disturbance is proven to have happened.
|CHK-ARSIZE
|Every aligned AxSIZE-0/1/2 cell returns exactly the addressed lanes of the primed word, and re-reads them identically after the complement-neighbour disturb, on beats where the responder legitimately drives nothing else.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Not claimed*: write-side strobe masking (`sep_axi_strobe_window_test`); AxSIZE=3 (that is the slice walk's full-width baseline).

'''''

[[sep_axi_map_refuse_test]]
===== `sep_axi_map_refuse_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Fabric and remap

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_axi_map_refuse_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Randomization*: RANDCFG — every directed anchor that is not on the exclude list runs on every seed, plus seed-selected addresses from the reserved rows. Excluded spans are skipped with a counted reason, and a row that could not yield its full quota is reported in the per-row shortfall line.

*Objective*: Rows marked reserved in the DV-owned table in `env/sep_axi_decode_map.py` allocate nothing, so an access there must not answer OKAY. The refusal expectation is taken from that table. Named reserved spans are excluded from the probe set with a counted reason. The walk grades refusal only, so any refusal passes and the code is logged; the code `sep.rdl` states is graded by `sep_unmapped_access_policy_test` at the points that test probes. Scope is the gaps BETWEEN block windows; `sep_fabric_deadspace_decode_test` walks the dead tail INSIDE one, and neither subsumes the other.

*Test Coverage*: Unmapped / refuse addresses complete with the specified error response.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Classify from the DV-owned table in `env/sep_axi_decode_map.py`; a reserved row, and any address in no row at all, allocates nothing. Inside the dual scratch bank (`0x1080_2000`-`0x1080_2FFF`), OTP (`0x1093_0000`-`0x1093_FFFF`) and system-bus (`0x10A0_0000`-`0x10A3_FFFF`) windows, each allocated row is one register block's decoded extent in `hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/py/sep_reg.py` (an RDL array spans all its elements at the array stride). Every span between those blocks is owned by no RDL block and is a reserved row. The OTP anchor is the first byte above the `EFUSE_MMR` block.
. Drop the named excluded spans from the probe set.
. Read and write the surviving anchors and the seed-selected reserved addresses, and report the per-row shortfall where a row yielded fewer probes than its quota.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-MAP-REFUSE
|Every probed address is refused. This checker grades refusal only, so DECERR and SLVERR both satisfy it and the line reports the split; the code `sep.rdl` states is graded by `sep_unmapped_access_policy_test` at the points that test probes. The directed anchors include the exact first byte above the generated boot-ROM extent; remaining probes come from map-reserved addresses this test asserts. Excluded spans are skipped before the walk with a counted reason.
|CHK-MAP-REFUSE-DATA
|A refused read returns none of the live words sampled on the same bus over the ordinary path: the live-bus control (`SEP_NMI_VEC`), `SW_RESET_N` (the block below the `0x10803008` anchor) and boot-ROM word 0 (the block below the one-past-ROM anchor). A refused access never reaches a unit (`hw/sys/sep/doc/memory_map.adoc`), so a refusal that hands back one of these values has reached its owner. Zero words are dropped from the set because they cannot discriminate. At least one refused read must be compared. Other live words are not claimed.
|CHK-NONVAC
|The DECERR beats the monitor observed on the bus equal the count the master reported, so the refusals are evidence from the pins and not only from the driver.
|CHK-OKAY
|A known-mapped CSR on the same bus returns OKAY and its generated reset value, read before any refusal probe. Without it a wedged or dead bus would refuse everything and read as a clean pass. The address and the expected value come from the generated SystemRDL export, and the register's reset is non-zero so the compare cannot pass against a tied-off decode.
|CHK-RANDCFG
|The probe and anchor counts for the seed are logged, so a shrunken walk is visible.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_fabric_deadspace_decode_test]]
===== `sep_fabric_deadspace_decode_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Fabric and remap

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_fabric_deadspace_decode_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Randomization*: RANDCFG — the twelve directed wrap offsets and six directed TRNG-window probes on every seed, plus two seed-selected dead offsets per block window.

*Objective*: Negative control on intra-block dead space. Each block owns an address window and populates only part of it; an access past the allocated size owns no register and must be refused. Window ends come from `hw/sys/sep/doc/memory_map.adoc`, which states the rule: the fabric refuses an address past the extent a unit allocates, and such an access never reaches a unit. Landing on a live register instead is a hard failure. A later beat of a burst that ends past the window's extent must be refused on the same terms as a single beat. The TRNG window, forwarded whole to an adopter endpoint and terminated in the reference integration, must refuse every access and alias no neighbouring ESRC register; its response code is logged for the document owner, not graded.

*Test Coverage*: Dead-space accesses are refused and do not alias live register data or state.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Each block owns an address window and populates only part of it.
. An access past the allocated size owns no register and must be refused.
. Window ends come from `hw/sys/sep/doc/memory_map.adoc`, which states the rule: the fabric refuses an address past the extent a unit allocates, and such an access never reaches a unit.
. A refusal that still writes the register is a failure, so every probe reads back the window's live registers as well as the response.
. A later beat of a burst that ends past the extent is held to the same rule as a single beat.
. The TRNG window (`TRNG_REG_MAP_BASE_ADDR` up to the lifecycle base) allocates no SEP register. Its first, middle and last word are read and written every seed, plus two seeded offsets. The no-alias snapshot for this window is the neighbouring ESRC register set, which an access that drops address bit 12 reaches.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-DEADSPACE-RAND
|Stimulus precondition. Each watched window supplies its full quota of two seed-selected, word-aligned dead-offset probes. No two probes, and no probe and directed anchor, share both address and operation. A window with no dead span counts as zero of two. The probe count is the random-coverage claim, so a short window fails the leaf before bring-up and is not absorbed into a smaller probe set. The checker has no PASS line; the `deadspace config` line records the probe count per seed.
|CHK-WINDOW-LIVE
|Each of the eleven watched windows (secure_dma, wdt_timer, aes, otbn, csrng, edn, entropy_src, km_mailbox, lifecycle, spi_controller, and trng against the neighbouring entropy_src registers) has a non-empty register snapshot and at least one register armed for the change compare, so both no-alias compares can fail. The line reports readable and armed separately: the per-probe change compare drops hardware-updating addresses, so armed is the falsifiable count, and a window with none armed fails.
|CHK-DEADSPACE-REFUSE
|Every probed address past the allocated size is refused (not OKAY), including the directed wrap anchors (KM `0x10920ff8`, DMA `0x108008a8`). A single accepted probe fails the checker; there is no tolerated count.
|CHK-DEADSPACE-NO-ALIAS
|No allocated register in that window changes across a probe, the directed wrap anchors included, and a read of a dead offset never returns a live register's value. The alias compare is not gated on the response: `memory_map.adoc` says an access past a unit's extent never reaches a unit, so a refused read that still hands back live data has reached it. Inside the extent the same passage grounds non-aliasing in exact-match register decode rather than in refusal, so an accepted read that returns a live value is the failure there. A single aliased probe fails the checker.
|CHK-DEADSPACE-BURST
|A burst that ends past the window's allocated extent and answers OKAY, where the same address is refused as a single beat, fails. A beat inside the extent is also compared against the value the single-beat path reads. A beat past the extent is held to the single-beat no-alias rule: its data must not equal a value in the window snapshot, whatever its response code, and every audited window must have its past-extent beat data compared. A burst that times out fails the audit rather than passing. A window whose dead space is 4KB-aligned, or that has no live words before it, carries no legal burst into it: it is named as not auditable rather than counted as a pass, and a run where no window was auditable fails.
|CHK-DEADSPACE-BEAT
|Each beat of the burst carries its own `RRESP`, kept in order by the bus monitor, so the refusal can be attributed to a beat rather than to the burst. No beat past a block extent may answer OKAY. The rule is asserted in that direction only: refusing the whole burst is legal AXI and stricter than the specification, so a window that refuses every beat is recorded, not failed. A second assertion requires at least one window that serves its in-extent beats and refuses only the tail — without one, the checker shows that nothing past an extent is served but not that the fabric can tell the beats apart. A captured vector whose length does not match the beats issued is treated as no evidence, since unrelated traffic on the bus would lengthen it. Read bursts only; see <<known-limitations>> for why writes cannot be.
|CHK-TRNG-UNOWNED
|Every TRNG-window probe is refused (never OKAY, no timeout), a refused read returns no neighbouring ESRC register value, and no ESRC register moves; at least one read and one write are refused. The reference integration connects no external TRNG, so no offset owns a register: `hw/sys/sep/doc/memory_map.adoc` requires SLVERR for an offset that owns no register, and `doc/integrator/src/smu.adoc` requires a DECERR slave when no external TRNG is connected. Both require a refusal, which is what is asserted. They name different flavours, so the read and write response codes are logged as `TRNG-UNOWNED-RESP` lines and in the PASS line for the document owner and are not graded. The window has no burst leg: its dead span starts on a 4 KB boundary.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_fabric_burst_wrap_test]]
===== `sep_fabric_burst_wrap_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Fabric and remap

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_fabric_burst_wrap_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Tracking*: #2493

*Randomization*: RAND-NONE — one directed write burst per block.

*Objective*: `hw/sys/sep/doc/memory_map.adoc` says the fabric refuses an address past the extent a unit allocates, and such an access never reaches a unit. The crossbar routes a burst on its first address, so a write burst that starts on a block's last live word carries its later beats past the extent into the same block. Those beats must not reach the unit, so no live register may change.

*Test Coverage*: `sep_reset_ctrl` (extent 8 B), `wdt_timer` (56 B) and `secure_dma` (336 B), extents from the generated SystemRDL export.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Snapshot every watched register of the block, the way `sep_fabric_deadspace_decode_test` does.
. Write the toggled value to the target register with a single beat, read it back, and restore it.
. Issue one INCR write burst from the last live word to `base + span + offset(target)`, where `span` is the smallest power of two above the allocated size. Beats inside the extent write back what they read, beats past it carry zero, and the final beat carries the toggled target value. Targets: `SW_RESET_N` (AES reset bit), `WKUP_THOLD_HI`, `SRC_ADDR_LO`.
. Read every watched register back and compare it with the snapshot. Restore on a change.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-WRAP-CONTROL
|The target register stores a single-beat write of the toggled value and returns to its original value, so the no-change compare can fail on it.
|CHK-WRAP-STIM
|The AW handshake on the testbench port is one INCR burst with the anchor's start address, AxLEN and AxSIZE, so the burst was not split into bursts that stay inside the extent.
|CHK-WRAP-NO-ALIAS
|After the burst every watched register of the block reads its snapshot value. A change is a burst beat past the extent that reached the unit, and fails whatever the response. The BRESP is logged, not graded: one BRESP covers the burst, and this checker grades what the burst reached, not the code. In all three blocks the burst changes the target register, so this checker fails.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. `UVM_ERROR` is 0.

*Current status*: red on the current RTL. `CHK-WRAP-NO-ALIAS` fails, and every other named checker PASSes. See <<known-limitations>>.

'''''

[[sep_unmapped_access_policy_test]]
===== `sep_unmapped_access_policy_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Fabric and remap

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_unmapped_access_policy_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Randomization*: RAND-NONE — every address is derived from the generated register export and walked on every run.

*Tracking*: #2503 (last array slot tail; fixed by #2586); #2592 (upper-half read word of a 32-bit `rdata`; stated by #2608); #2504, #2505 (per-unit refusal code); #2489, #2490, #2495 (behaviour evidence)

*Objective*: The SEP components view of the SystemRDL memory map (`hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/py/sep_memory_map.py`, generated from `hw/sys/sep/regs/sep.rdl` by `tools/regs/rdlmap.py`, the same model that renders `hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/adoc/memory_map.adoc`) gives each unit's Decoded Extent and states how a 32-bit access that no register backs answers, as seen at the SEP inbound port: in a hole inside the extent, past the extent, and in a reserved row between apertures. Each response gives the read response, the RDL read data and the write response; a row's RDL note gives the write code in named reserved ranges. `doc/trm/src/memory_map.adoc` (Decode Response Codes) defines the cells. This leaf holds the DUT to that table at the points that sit next to live registers and that `sep_fabric_deadspace_decode_test` and `sep_axi_map_refuse_test` do not probe. `env/sep_decode_resp.py` imports that view, so no expected code is typed into the test. `rdata` is a 64-bit field in `sep.rdl`: its low word answers a read with address bit 2 clear and its upper word a read with address bit 2 set. A 32-bit RDL rdata (no upper word) is the word a 32-bit read returns on either half of the bus (`ocah_resp.rdata` in `hw/common/regs/regblock_udps.rdl`; Decode Response Codes in `doc/trm/src/memory_map.adoc`). The expected codes and read data are the `ocah_resp` values `sep.rdl` states. `sep.rdl` is the specification of record for these codes, so `CHK-UNMAPPED-CODE` holds the DUT to the specification. Where the map and the RDL allocation of an array disagree, `CHK-ARRAY-TAIL` grades against the RDL allocation.

*Test Coverage*: System-CSR holes, the reserved row above SEP CPU control, the mailbox page's upper half, the space past each eFuse sibling block and past the SPI host, the end of the eFuse token MMR extent, and every remap and filter array slot tail answer the response code and read data the map states, and none of them aliases a live register.

*Scope*:

* System-CSR holes: the gaps between the AP and STEE output-remap arrays, after the STEE array, between the outbound and inbound filter arrays, before SEP CPU control, past the SEP CPU control extent, and one SEP CPU control image up.
* Reserved row: the generated map row from the end of the SEP CPU control aperture to the external IO bridge, first and last word of each 64 KiB page. The row has two refusal paths, and the log records which one each probe takes. The system-peripherals crossbar sends the `0x10A4_0000` page to the system-CSR AXI-Lite port, and that port's error slave refuses it. The local crossbar still sends `0x10A5_0000` and above into its system-peripherals window. The system-peripherals crossbar sends those addresses back to the local crossbar on its SMN-inbound output. The local crossbar does not connect that initiator to the system-peripherals target, so its error slave answers, and the access never reaches the system-CSR AXI-Lite port.
* Mailbox upper half: from `AXIL_MAILBOX` base plus its size rounded up to a power of two (`0x10A0_8000`) to the end of the 64 KiB mailbox aperture. All of it is past the mailbox's Decoded Extent. The probes include the images of outbound mailbox 0 `WRITE_DATA`, `WIRQT`, `RIRQT` and `STATUS` and of the last inbound mailbox `WIRQT`.
* eFuse: past `efuse_interface_ctrl` (28 B extent) and past `efuse_mmr` (116 B extent), each to the end of its 256 B window, including the image of a live register one extent up and half a window up. Past `efuse_interface_ctrl`, reads cover both 32-bit lanes of the 64-bit bus (address bit 2 set and clear).
* SPI host: past `spi_controller` (56 B extent) to the end of its 1 MiB aperture: the first two words past the extent (both bus lanes), the `CSID` image one extent up, the `CONFIGOPTS` image half an aperture up, and the last word of the aperture.
* Array tails: every word between the end of a slot's registers and the next slot, for the 16 alias-remap, 32 outbound-filter and 16 inbound-filter slots. The array extent is the array base plus `SEP_TOP_<ARRAY>_TOTAL_SIZE` from the generated `hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/c/sep_addr.h` (`0x200`, `0x400` and `0x200`): the RDL allocates the full slot stride to every slot, the last one included. That size must equal the aperture of the array's map row. Every tail inside the extent, the last slot's tail included, must read zero and discard a write with OKAY. `sep.rdl` sets `ocah_full_stride_extent` on these arrays, so the map row's Decoded Extent is the full allocation and the map states OKAY with zero for every tail, the last one included. The first word past the outbound and inbound filter apertures (`0x10A2_0400`, `0x10A2_1200`) is in a reserved row and must be refused. The first word past the alias-remap array is `AP_OUTPUT_REMAP_CTRL[0]`, a live word, so it is not a refusal point.
* The Key Manager's own view of the token MMR (`0x0001_1570`) needs KM firmware and is not probed here.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Program a distinct non-zero value into one live word of every array slot, the first and last AP / STEE output-remap entries, `SEP_NMI_VEC`, three mailbox thresholds, both eFuse timeouts, two eFuse token inputs and the SPI host `CSID`, and read each back. `CSID` takes effect only when a command is issued, and this leaf issues none. Each programmed word is read before its write, for the restore, except a word with a field that has no RDL reset. The two eFuse token inputs are such words: `hw/ip/efuse/regs/efuse_mmr.rdl` gives the `token` field of `RMA_TOKEN_I` and `SEC_DISABLE_TOKEN_I` no reset value, so the register has no value before its first write. The test reads this from the generated IP-XACT (`hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/ipxact/sep.xml`, no `resets` element on the field), not from the generated `_REG_DEFAULT`, which reads 0 for a field without a reset. These words are written before any read of them. Snapshot every other word of those slots, every mailbox `STATUS`, `TOKEN_MATCH_FAULT` and the SPI host `CONFIGOPTS`.
. Read `TOKEN_MATCH_FAULT` through its generated symbol.
. Read and write each probe with 32-bit singles. After each write, re-read every programmed word and the non-array snapshot words. For system-CSR and reserved-row probes, record whether the access reached the system-CSR AXI-Lite port.
. Read and write each array slot tail and the first word past each filter array, and after each write re-read the words of that slot and of the next slot. After each write to a tail inside the array extent, read that tail again.
. Re-read every live word, then restore the programmed words that were read before their write. The eFuse token inputs are not restored.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-UNMAPPED-LIVE
|Every programmed word reads back its value with OKAY, and every snapshot word reads OKAY. A word with a field that has no RDL reset is written before its first read; the config does not build if a snapshot-only word has such a field. This is the positive control: it shows the bus reaches every block that owns a probed hole, and the distinct values are what make an alias visible.
|CHK-SYSCSR-HOLE-REFUSE
|Every read and write in the system-CSR holes is refused: not OKAY, and not a timeout. For this checker and the five below it, the config does not build unless the map states a refusal at every probe in the group.
|CHK-SYS-RESERVED-REFUSE
|Every read and write in the reserved row above SEP CPU control is refused, the `0x10A5_0000` page included.
|CHK-MBOX-UPPER-REFUSE
|Every read and write in the upper half of the mailbox page is refused.
|CHK-EFUSE-CTRL-PAST-REFUSE
|Every read and write past the `efuse_interface_ctrl` extent is refused.
|CHK-EFUSE-MMR-PAST-REFUSE
|Every read and write past the `efuse_mmr` extent is refused.
|CHK-SPI-PAST-REFUSE
|Every read and write past the `spi_controller` extent, to the end of its aperture, is refused.
|CHK-TOKEN-FAULT-EXTENT
|`TOKEN_MATCH_FAULT`, the last register `hw/ip/efuse/regs/efuse_mmr.rdl` places in the token MMR (`@0x70`), reads OKAY with its RDL reset value, and the first word after it (`EFUSE_MMR` base + `REG_MAP_SIZE`) is refused. The extent therefore ends at `0x1093_0573`, where the RDL ends it. That the register holds live fault state is graded by `sep_efuse_rma_token_rand_test`, which reads a raised fault bit through the same address.
|CHK-ARRAY-TAIL
|The array extent is the array base + `SEP_TOP_<ARRAY>_TOTAL_SIZE`, the RDL allocation. Every tail inside it, the last slot's tail included, answers OKAY, and a read of it returns zero. A re-read of such a tail after its write answers OKAY with zero, so the write was discarded. The first word past the extent is refused where no other RDL block owns it. The config does not build unless the array's map row spans exactly that extent and the map states a refusal for every word past it. A tail inside the extent where the map states anything other than OKAY with zero is a map disagreement: it is graded here only and logged as `UNMAPPED-MAP-DISAGREE`. A tail write never changes a word of its own slot or of the next slot. The walk must grade at least one word of each kind. A failure names the tail address, the response and the extent.
|CHK-UNMAPPED-CODE
|Every probe and tail access that completes, except the map-disagree tail words, answers the response code the map states for its address and channel, and every read returns the stated read data: the low word of the RDL rdata, or its upper word when address bit 2 is set. The codes and read data are the `ocah_resp` values `sep.rdl` states. A 32-bit RDL rdata is graded as that word on both bus halves. A write in a range the write-code note names answers that note's code. Past `efuse_interface_ctrl` and `spi_controller`, reads cover both 32-bit bus lanes. Every refusal read is issued inside the monitor's error-read-data window, so an X or Z in an error payload fails the run instead of reading as zero; every error-response read must be lane-checked. A failure names the address, the response and read data seen, and the map row, column and cell. The walk must grade at least one access.
|CHK-UNMAPPED-NO-ALIAS
|No refused read returns the value of a programmed word. No probe write changes a watched live word. A closing re-read of every live word matches the snapshot.
|CHK-UNMAPPED-PINS
|The DECERR beats the bus monitor saw equal the DECERR responses the master reported, and the run reported at least one.
|===

The `UNMAPPED-CODE` lines tally the codes `CHK-UNMAPPED-CODE` graded, per group and channel, and record which system-CSR and reserved-row probes reached the system-CSR AXI-Lite port.

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. `UVM_ERROR` is 0.

*Current status*: green on the current RTL.

'''''

[[sep_efuse_set_only_monotonicity_test]]
===== `sep_efuse_set_only_monotonicity_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: eFuse, lifecycle, SEC_DIS, locked-field

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`; omit `+skip_fuse_sense` so the shadow bank is live

*Source Module*: `sep_efuse_set_only_monotonicity_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: Proves the eFuse shadow-register write path on every `otp_fuse_controller.adoc` fuse-field row except `LC_STATE`: set-only rows OR-merge so a bit that is already set cannot be cleared by a write of zero, and SW-writable rows overwrite. Randomization is RANDCFG — every walked row is visited every seed, word index and bit patterns come from the run seed, and the write-policy contract itself is directed.

*Test Coverage*: Every walked fuse-field row follows its specification write policy. The lifecycle nibble stays with the LC W1S leaves.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Scope is the shadow-register write path.
. Every `hw/sys/sep/doc/otp_fuse_controller.adoc` fuse-field row except `LC_STATE` is walked. Set-only rows OR-merge. SW-writable `true` rows overwrite, then a write of 0 clears.
. `LOCK` is both RDL windows: `LOCKS` word 0, `LOCKS` word 1, and `LOCKS_SPARE`. Assigned lock slots stay 0 at sense and are written after every other row so they do not write-lock a field this invocation still writes. `LOCKS_SPARE` stages ones only in the unassigned half; the assigned half is set from zero in that same late cell.
. `SIP_DIS` and `SYS_DIS` stay on their function-group word.
. Program × lock is a different mechanism.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-SENSE
|The staged value is read back. Staged set-only words carry a known 1. Assigned `LOCKS` words sense 0.
|CHK-CLEAR-REJECT
|A frontdoor write of 0 leaves a sensed 1. Assigned `LOCKS` words start at 0, so this cell is not claimed there; `LOCKS_SPARE` claims it on the unassigned half.
|CHK-SET-STICKS
|A write that sets a previously-0 bit sticks; readback is the OR. Both `LOCKS` words and the assigned `LOCKS_SPARE` half take this cell from zero.
|CHK-OR-MERGE
|A later write of 0 cannot clear the OR.
|CHK-WRITABLE-CONTRAST
|Every SW-writable row overwrites, then a write of 0 clears, so the OR is field-local.
|CHK-RANDCFG
|Forty-one fuse-field rows walked in one invocation: six early set-only cells (`SIP_DIS`, `SYS_DIS`, `CHIPLET_PUBK_REVOKE`, `BL1_VERSION`, `BL2_VERSION`, `REQUIRED_ALGS`), three late `LOCK` cells (`LOCKS` word 0, `LOCKS` word 1, `LOCKS_SPARE`), and the 34 SW-writable rows. `LOCK` is counted only when both RDL windows are written. `LC_STATE` is not this leaf. `SIP_DIS` and `SYS_DIS` stay on their function-group word so the walk does not OR `sep_debug`, `chiplet_dbg` or `sip_debug` into `FEAT_CTRL`. This test drives the CPU-LSU master, which has no inbound filter.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_esrc_fips_lock_test]]
===== `sep_esrc_fips_lock_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_esrc_fips_lock_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, entropy golden

*Randomization*: RANDCFG — every certified-configuration class every seed; pre-lock value and rejected poke come from the run seed, and each field's poke is selected to differ from that field's pre-lock value.

*Objective*: The FIPS lock makes ESRC configuration write-once: a pre-lock write moves the field off reset, the same field rejects a poke after `LOCK=1`, write-0 cannot clear the lock, and `rst_ni` is the only way back, with the BIW observe enable still writable under the lock. The advisory RCT/APT cutoffs derived from `MIN_ENTROPY_H` are graded against an SP 800-90B oracle. Scope: health-test *quality* is not claimed.

*Test Coverage*: FIPS lock and the matching entropy-source status.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. One of two vehicles on the ESRC lock-and-alert scorecard row.
. Health-test *quality* is not claimed.
. `FIPS_LOCK.LOCK` is write-1-only.
. Observe FIFOs stay writable under lock.
. Walks every field `entropy_source.rdl` marks `swwel` -- the
certified-configuration inventory `FIPS_LOCK` freezes -- one target per
register, except `CTRL.MODULE_ENABLE`. The sequence declares its coverage and
reconciles it against that RDL inventory at import, so a lock added or dropped
there fails the test instead of leaving the walk quietly short.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-PRE-LOCK
|A pre-lock write moves the field off reset.
|CHK-POST-LOCK
|The same field rejects a poke after `LOCK=1`.
|CHK-LOCK-SET
|`FIPS_LOCK.LOCK` reads 1 after the arming write.
|CHK-LOCK-W1S
|Write-0 leaves `FIPS_LOCK.LOCK=1`.
|CHK-CTRL-RSVD
|Reserved `CTRL.RSVD0` reads 0, and writing it before the lock leaves `CTRL`
unchanged, where the other `CTRL` fields are demonstrably writable so an
unchanged register isolates `RSVD0`. Repeated after the lock, the same write
leaves `FIPS_LOCK.LOCK=1`.
|CHK-RST-NI
|`rst_ni` clears `FIPS_LOCK.LOCK` to 0.
|CHK-POST-UNLOCK
|After `rst_ni` the released target reads its register reset, then takes the
poke that the lock rejected. The target is chosen so its poke differs from the
reset, otherwise the reset value alone would satisfy the readback.
|CHK-OBS-ENABLE-WRITABLE
|`BIW_OBS_CTRL.RAW_ENABLE` stays writable under lock. This is the control bit
only. `BIW_OBS_STATUS` and `BIW_OBS_RDATA` are not read, so the observe FIFO
itself is not claimed live.
|CHK-REC-THRESH
|`RECOMMENDED_THRESHOLDS.RCT_LIMIT` tracks `MIN_ENTROPY_H` across a sweep of
eight Q4.4 points, compared against SP 800-90B 4.4.1 `C = 1 + ceil(20/H)`
re-derived in the test. The oracle is the standard, not
`entropy_source_rec_thresh_lut.sv`, which the checker never reads. `APT_LIMIT` is
an exact binomial cutoff with no closed form; what is claimed for it is that it
stays inside the 1024-sample window and falls as the assessed min-entropy rises.
|CHK-RANDCFG
|26 locked registers walked in one invocation, covering 34 of the 35 fields
`entropy_source.rdl` marks `swwel`. Every register that locks two
fields moves both off reset in the pre-lock write, so a field inside the compare
mask is never left unwritten.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Not claimed*: `CTRL.MODULE_ENABLE`. It is locked too, but clearing it stops the
block for the rest of the walk, so its lock belongs to the reset-recovery
vehicle.

'''''

[[sep_esrc_alert_delivery_test]]
===== `sep_esrc_alert_delivery_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`, `+esrc_noise_force`

*Source Module*: `sep_esrc_alert_delivery_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, entropy golden

*Randomization*: RAND-NONE — directed persistent-failure trip.

*Objective*: A short window plus stuck forced noise trips `ALERT_THRESHOLD`,
and the alert bookkeeping the RDL defines is read at that trip. An opening
`INTR_TEST` sweep, before the trip, drives every interrupt source the RDL defines.

*Test Coverage*: Entropy-source alerts reach status and the aggregator.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Second vehicle on the same scorecard row.
. From the clean pre-trip state, pulse each `INTR_TEST` source on its own.
. A short window plus stuck forced noise trips `ALERT_THRESHOLD`.
. Read the alert counters and the health-test latch at the trip.
. Health-test *quality* is not claimed.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-NO-ALERT
|ALERT, PERSISTENT_FAILURE, and PIC source 16 are 0 before the trip.
|CHK-INTR-TEST
|Each `INTR_TEST` source `ENTROPY_SOURCE.fields("INTR_TEST")` names sets only
its own `INTR_STATUS` bit, raises PIC source 16, and clears on W1C. Swept
before the trip: `INTR_STATUS` bits are re-driven from their live cause, so a
source whose real condition is latched cannot clear. The expected count is
that field list, not a literal.
|CHK-ALERT
|`MAIN_SM_STATUS.ALERT` asserts after the threshold.
|CHK-PERSISTENT-FAILURE
|`INTR_STATUS.PERSISTENT_FAILURE` asserts. Note it is the OR of the AlertHang
trip, a main-FSM error and a counter-integrity error; CHK-SM-ERR-ZERO is what
makes this one attributable.
|CHK-PIC-16
|`sep_internal_interrupts[15]` (PIC source 16) asserts. Only PERSISTENT_FAILURE
is enabled for the trip, so this adds the aggregate slot wiring on top of
CHK-PERSISTENT-FAILURE rather than an independent cause.
|CHK-SM-ERR-ZERO
|`MAIN_SM_STATUS.ERR` stays 0 across the trip, so PERSISTENT_FAILURE is
attributable to the alert threshold.
|CHK-ANY-FAIL-WINDOWS
|`ALERT_SUMMARY_FAIL_COUNTS.ANY_FAIL_COUNT` sits inside `[ALERT_THRESHOLD,
ALERT_THRESHOLD + 8]` at the trip and stays at or below the summed per-lane fail
pulses. `ALERT_THRESHOLD` is 3: at 1 the alert comparator already implies the
lower bound, and no denomination is distinguishable at a count of one. The upper
bound against the per-lane pulse total is what rejects a counter denominated in
events rather than in failing *windows*.
|CHK-FAIL-ATTRIB
|`REPCNT_TOTAL_FAILS` is non-zero, attributing the trip to the repetition test
under stuck noise with `REPETITION_LIMIT` = 5, and each lane's 32-bit total
covers its 4-bit `ALERT_FAIL_COUNTS` counter. The two take the same fail pulse
but carry different widths and different clears, so the compare is a
DUT-to-DUT contract. The reverse does not hold and is not claimed:
`alert_cntrs_clr` also fires on a passing window.
|CHK-HT-STATUS
|`HEALTH_TEST_STATUS.HEALTH_STATUS` latches a failing test while the alert is
asserted. No reserved bit latches: `entropy_source.rdl` allocates
repetition [0], APT [3], Markov high [4] and Markov low [5], leaving [1:2] and
[6:7] reserved. After `HEALTH_TEST_CTRL.ENABLE=0` the latch still holds the trip
bits, and a write of one to every latched bit reads back 0.
|CHK-GEN-HEALTH
|All twelve `GENERATOR_n_HEALTH_STATUS` registers are read at the trip. At least
one latches a failing lane, and none latches a reserved bit.
|CHK-W1C
|After leaving AlertHang, W1C clears both bits and the PIC line.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Not claimed*: `es_cntr_err` -> `MAIN_SM_STATUS.ERR`. This is the health-test
counter-redundancy countermeasure: `prim_count` holds each count twice, up and
down, and reports an error only when `cnt_q[0] + cnt_q[1]` stops equalling the
counter maximum. No stimulus can break that invariant — both copies take the same
enable and the same step — so the path is reachable by a physical fault and by
nothing else. Proving it therefore requires fault injection, which no vehicle
in `all` performs — see <<phase-3>>.
CHK-SM-ERR-ZERO proves the bit is 0 on the alert path, which is what makes
PERSISTENT_FAILURE attributable to the threshold trip; it does not claim the
error path works.

'''''

[[sep_sec_dis_override_test]]
===== `sep_sec_dis_override_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: eFuse, lifecycle, SEC_DIS, locked-field

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`; omit `+skip_fuse_sense`; `+sep_edn_jtag_reset_waive`. The JTAG `sep_reset_n` override resets the crypto engines without isolation, so an engine can drop an ungranted EDN request with no endpoint cancel; `tb_top` turns off the crypto EDN arbiter and adapter request-hold assertions for this leaf.

*Source Module*: `sep_sec_dis_override_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Randomization*: RAND-NONE — directed match / mismatch / restore.

*Objective*: A match is reachable because `tb_top` binds `SEP_SEC_DISABLE_TOKEN` to SHA-256 of the all-zero token. `hw/sys/sep/doc/security_disable.adoc` says a match does not release the SEP reset, and that the IC_RESET `sep_reset_n` override is an independent step: the override alone releases the reset and leaves life-cycle control closed. `hw/sys/sep/doc/token_processing.adoc` says the match opens feature control without waiting for fuse sensing. `hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc` records the override. This leaf drives the SEP override inputs and reads the reset probes and `FEAT_CTRL`. The bus access and the IC_RESET TDR write are `sep_sec_dis_reset_reach_test`. A read of the `LC_STATE` shadow-map word while SEC_DIS is active and sensing is still open must return AXI OKAY. The map gate input is tied off, so that read returns an error and the leaf fails there.

*Test Coverage*: Security-disable match, mismatch, restore, override-before-match, sense-gated reset hold, pre-sense feature control, the same override inputs after the match, and the pre-sense shadow-map read.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. A match is reachable because `tb_top` binds `SEP_SEC_DISABLE_TOKEN` to SHA-256 of the all-zero token (`CHK-MATCH` is the bind proof).
. The comparator hashes the written `SEC_DISABLE_TOKEN_I` and compares it to the metal expected digest (`hw/sys/sep/doc/token_processing.adoc`).
. After `release_no_cpu_reset`, and before any token, drive `jtag_sep_reset_n_ovrd_i=1` and `jtag_sep_reset_n_val_i=1`. Both reset probes read 1 while `sep_fuse_sense_done_o` is still 0 and `sec_dis` is 0. `FEAT_CTRL` equals the withheld-map golden (invalid lifecycle, feature control closed). Clear both inputs. Both probes read 0.
. Write the all-zero token before `sep_fuse_sense_done_o`. Match must assert, and `sep_cpu_reset_n_o` and `dbg_sep_reset_n_o` must stay 0 while sense is still open (`ext_boot_seq_done_i` is already 1, so sense is the only held term). `FEAT_CTRL` equals the all-ones override golden.
. Drive the override again. Both reset probes read 1 while sense is still 0 and `sec_dis` is 1. Clear both inputs. Both probes read 0.
. Read the `LC_STATE` shadow-map word while `sec_dis=1` and sense is still 0. The checker requires AXI OKAY.
. Wait for sense-done. Token `1` mismatches.
. On a later match, `sec_dis` asserts and `FEAT_CTRL` follows feat_ctrl_expected(..., sec_dis=1).

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-OVR-ALONE
|Before any match, with `sep_fuse_sense_done_o=0` and `sec_dis=0`, `jtag_sep_reset_n_ovrd_i=1` and `jtag_sep_reset_n_val_i=1` make both reset probes read 1, and `FEAT_CTRL` equals the withheld-map golden (invalid lifecycle, closed). Clearing the override makes both probes read 0. `hw/sys/sep/doc/security_disable.adoc`: the override alone releases the reset and leaves life-cycle control closed.
|CHK-SENSE-GATED-RESET
|A match with `sep_fuse_sense_done_o=0` and `ext_boot_seq_done_i=1` leaves `sep_cpu_reset_n_o` and `dbg_sep_reset_n_o` at 0. `hw/sys/sep/doc/security_disable.adoc`: a match does not release the SEP reset.
|CHK-FEAT-PRESENSE
|With that match still held and `sep_fuse_sense_done_o=0`, `sec_dis=1` and `FEAT_CTRL` equals the all-ones golden, and both reset probes stay 0. `hw/sys/sep/doc/token_processing.adoc`: the feature-control override does not wait for fuse sensing.
|CHK-OVR-AFTER
|With `sec_dis=1` and `sep_fuse_sense_done_o=0`, `jtag_sep_reset_n_ovrd_i=1` and `jtag_sep_reset_n_val_i=1` make both reset probes read 1. Clearing the override makes both probes read 0 while sense is still 0. The IC_RESET TDR write is `sep_sec_dis_reset_reach_test`.
|CHK-MAP-OPEN
|With `sec_dis=1` and `sep_fuse_sense_done_o=0`, a read of the `LC_STATE` shadow-map word returns AXI OKAY. The map gate input is tied off, so the read returns an error and this checker fails.
|CHK-MISMATCH
|Written token=1 returns `SEC_DISABLE_TOKEN_MATCH=0x2a`.
|CHK-NO-OVERRIDE
|After the mismatch, `sec_dis` stays 0; `FEAT_CTRL` equals the fail-closed PROD golden.
|CHK-MATCH
|Written all-zero token returns `SEC_DISABLE_TOKEN_MATCH=0x15`.
|CHK-OVERRIDE
|After the match, sec_dis=1 and `FEAT_CTRL` equals the override golden (all ones).
|CHK-DEACTIVATE
|A later token=1 returns mismatch again.
|CHK-RESTORE
|After that mismatch, `sec_dis=0` and `FEAT_CTRL` is fail-closed PROD again.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. `UVM_ERROR` is 0.

*Current status*: red on the current RTL. `CHK-MAP-OPEN` fails, and every other named checker PASSes. See <<known-limitations>>.

'''''

[[sep_sec_dis_reset_reach_test]]
===== `sep_sec_dis_reset_reach_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: eFuse, lifecycle, SEC_DIS, locked-field

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`; omit `+skip_fuse_sense`; `+sep_edn_jtag_reset_waive`. The JTAG `sep_reset_n` override resets the crypto engines without isolation, so an engine can drop an ungranted EDN request with no endpoint cancel; `tb_top` turns off the crypto EDN arbiter and adapter request-hold assertions for this leaf.

*Source Module*: `sep_sec_dis_reset_reach_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, `jtag_ic_reset_reg`

*Randomization*: RAND-NONE — the eFuse image is PROD with fixed `SIP_DIS` / `SYS_DIS`

*Objective*: `hw/sys/sep/doc/security_disable.adoc` says the IC_RESET `sep_reset_n` override alone releases the SEP reset and leaves life-cycle control closed. `hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc` says JTAG drives `sep_reset_n` through the SEP slice of the IC_RESET TDR. This leaf shifts `jtag_ic_reset_reg`, sized to `jtag_sep_reset_ctrl_t` and packed the way `jtag_ptap` packs the SEP slice. The TAP instruction that selects the TDR is outside this DUT. The per-port override pins stay 0. `SW_RESET_N` is in the `sep_reset_n` domain (`hw/sys/sep/rtl/sep_reset_ctrl.sv`). No token is written.

*Test Coverage*: TDR reset value, control-then-enable programming, `SW_RESET_N` readback, closed feature control, TDR re-hold, sense completing the release, and `SW_RESET_N` readback after that release.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. After `release_no_cpu_reset`, both reset probes read 0 and `sep_fuse_sense_done_o` is 0.
. Reset the TDR and select it. Both probes stay 0. The override pins stay 0.
. Capture-DR and shift the reset value back in. The shifted-out word is all ones. Update-DR leaves both probes at 0.
. Shift again with `sep_reset_n` `reset_enable` cleared and `reset_control` held at 1. Both probes read 1 while sensing is still open. The override pins stay 0.
. Read `SW_RESET_N`. The access returns AXI OKAY and the register reset default.
. `FEAT_CTRL` equals the withheld-map golden and `sec_dis` stays 0.
. Shift the all-ones word again. Both probes read 0 while sensing is still open.
. Wait for sense-done. Both probes read 1 with the TDR override off.
. Read `SW_RESET_N` again. The access returns AXI OKAY and the register reset default.
. Read `FEAT_CTRL`. It equals the PROD golden of the pinned image.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-TDR-IDLE
|Selecting the TDR at its reset value, with `sep_fuse_sense_done_o=0`, leaves both reset probes at 0 on every clock of the release bound CHK-TDR-RELEASE uses. `jtag_sep_reset_n_ovrd_i` and `jtag_sep_reset_n_val_i` stay 0.
|CHK-TDR-CAPTURE
|The word shifted out of the TDR after reset is all ones: `reset_hold` and every `reset_enable` and `reset_control` are 1.
|CHK-TDR-CTRL
|An Update-DR that rewrites that reset value leaves both probes at 0 on every clock of the same release bound. `reset_control` moves without `reset_enable`.
|CHK-TDR-RELEASE
|Clearing `reset_enable` for `sep_reset_n`, with `reset_control` at 1, makes both probes read 1 while `sep_fuse_sense_done_o=0`. The override pins stay 0.
|CHK-REACH
|That read of `SW_RESET_N` returns AXI OKAY and the register reset default, while sensing is still open and both probes are 1.
|CHK-FEAT-CLOSED
|With no token match, `sec_dis=0` and `FEAT_CTRL` equals the withheld-map golden. `hw/sys/sep/doc/security_disable.adoc`: the override alone leaves life-cycle control closed.
|CHK-TDR-REHOLD
|Restoring `reset_enable` makes both probes read 0 while sensing is still open.
|CHK-SENSE-RELEASE
|After sense-done, with the TDR override off, both probes read 1.
|CHK-SENSE-REACH
|After that release, a read of `SW_RESET_N` returns AXI OKAY and the register reset default, with both probes at 1.
|CHK-FEAT-SENSED
|After sense-done, `FEAT_CTRL` equals the PROD golden of the pinned image (`SIP_DIS` and `SYS_DIS` fixed), which differs from the withheld-map golden. It is the positive control for CHK-FEAT-CLOSED: the same register leaves the withheld value once sensing completes.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. `UVM_ERROR` is 0.

'''''

[[sep_fabric_local_alias_datapath_test]]
===== `sep_fabric_local_alias_datapath_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Fabric and remap

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_fabric_local_alias_datapath_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Randomization*: RANDCFG — region and source page from the run seed; the dest is `CLOCK_GATE_CTRL` so the offset is determined and non-vacuous.

*Objective*: Scope is the sixteen programmable local-master regions ahead of the system-peripherals routing demux (`hw/sys/sep/doc/fabric.adoc`, "Address Remapping").

*Test Coverage*: Local-alias remap datapath, not the CSR bank alone.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Distinct from the CPU high-alias window (`sep_cpu_ifu_lsu_alias_remap_matrix_test`).
. Scope is the sixteen programmable local-master regions ahead of the system-peripherals routing demux (`hw/sys/sep/doc/fabric.adoc`, "Address Remapping").
. CSR programming stays on `sep_fabric_remap_filter_csr_bank_test`.
. Park `CLOCK_GATE_CTRL` at the inverse of the marker, program the region, and write the marker through the alias; the identity `CLOCK_GATE_CTRL` readback must move to the marker. Then change `CLOCK_GATE_CTRL` at its identity address only; the aliased read must follow it.
. Local-master `REGION_ATTRS` has `valid` and `cacheable`.
. Cacheable overrides AXI `cache` on a hit; this vehicle does not observe that field.
. The `ext_debug_bus_o` lane is logged after the aliased write and read as information only. No RDL, generated header or document defines its layout, so no checker decodes it.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-OFFSET-WRITE
|An aliased write of the marker moves the identity `CLOCK_GATE_CTRL` readback from a parked value (the marker with every implemented bit inverted) to the marker. A write that lands on any other address leaves the parked value.
|CHK-OFFSET
|A programmed region's live read equals input plus the programmed offset. The aliased read returns the marker, then returns the parked value after a write to `CLOCK_GATE_CTRL` at its identity address only. The source page's own value differs from the marker, so a read that passes through, or lands on any other register, cannot match both.
|CHK-VALID-GATE
|The region valid bit gates the remap: `hw/sys/sep/doc/fabric.adoc` ("Address Remapping") states that an invalid entry behaves as a transparent pass-through. The same window is reprogrammed with bounds and offset live and only the enable clear, and the beat must then read the value at the address the master issued rather than the remapped destination. Programming the window first is what separates a working gate from an address that was never in range, and the vacuity guard on `CHK-OFFSET` has already shown the two values differ. The cacheable flag that `fabric.adoc` lists per region is not checked: this bench has no observable on the outbound cache bits.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_km_isolate_termination_test]]
===== `sep_km_isolate_termination_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Key Manager vault

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`, `+km_rom_hex=km_rom_iso.parhex`

*Source Module*: `sep_km_isolate_termination_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, KM CPU image

*Randomization*: RAND-NONE — one directed path, three directed legs.

*Objective*: What a Key Manager master transaction receives when its own AXI-Lite path to a destination is isolated. Every other isolation entry in this plan drives the SEP host port, which reaches the `host_*` paths and never the `km_*` ones, so the response a KM transaction gets was not graded anywhere. The KM CPU image stores to the same HMAC wrapper key address three times: clean while the path is open, an AXI error while `km_hmac` is isolated, clean again after release.

*Test Coverage*: `km_hmac` isolate termination, and that isolation does not wedge the KM path.

*Functional Coverage*: `sep_crypto_isolate_cg` in link:SEP_FCOV.adoc[SEP_FCOV]. This entry is the only leaf that fills all three bins — it isolates a KM path, presents a KM master transaction to it, and reopens it. The bins are edges rather than states, because both isolate bits hold high for the whole reset window and a state bin would fill even on a run whose reset preceded the drain.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Dedicated `km_rom_iso` on the KM CPU bus, not `rom_main`. The image publishes a phase marker in KM SRAM word0 and blocks on an inbound mailbox word between legs, so the host controls when each leg runs.
. Each leg W1Cs `IRQ_STATUS` AXI_SLVERR and AXI_DECERR and requires them to read back clear before its store. The bits are sticky, so without that precondition a leg would also be satisfied by an earlier fault anywhere on the KM CPU bus.
. Leg A with HMAC released: the store completes with neither bit set.
. The host parks HMAC by read-modify-write of `SW_RESET_N`, preserving the Key Manager's own release bit, then waits for the `km_hmac` isolate-completion bit before releasing the image into leg B. A leg B that raced the coordinator would store into a still-open path.
. Leg B: the same store raises AXI_SLVERR or AXI_DECERR.
. The host releases HMAC; leg C repeats the store and requires it clean.

[NOTE]
====
Legs A and C cannot fail on their own — "no error" is also what a dead poll loop
reports — and are not offered as evidence by themselves. They are the controls
that make `CHK-KM-ISO-TERM` discriminating: the same instruction at the same
address in the same image, differing only in whether the host holds HMAC in
reset. Without them the error leg would be satisfied by an address that always
errors.
====

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,6",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |What it proves
|CHK-KM-ISO-PRECOND
|Every leg began with both AXI error bits clear, so the bit a leg reports belongs to that leg's store.
|CHK-KM-PATH-LIVE
|With HMAC released, the KM store to the HMAC key aperture completes with no AXI error — the path is open.
|CHK-KM-ISO-TERM
|With `km_hmac` isolated, the same store raises AXI_SLVERR or AXI_DECERR. It neither completes nor hangs.
|CHK-KM-ISO-REOPEN
|After release the same store is clean again, so isolation did not leave the KM path wedged.
|===
*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Not claimed*: the other five KM destination paths (`km_otbn`, `km_aes`,
`km_kmac`, `km_abr`, `km_efuse`). They share the mechanism; only `km_hmac` is
demonstrated. The response code is graded as "an AXI error", not pinned to
SLVERR specifically, because the KM CSR status bit the image polls reports the
two codes as one field.

'''''

[[sep_km_ic_reset_release_test]]
===== `sep_km_ic_reset_release_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Key Manager vault

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`, `+km_rom_hex=km_rom.parhex`. VCS only (`tools = ["vcs"]` in `testlists/km.toml`).

*Source Module*: `sep_km_ic_reset_release_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, IC_RESET TDR (`jtag_ic_reset_reg` in `tb_top`), KM CPU image

*Randomization*: RAND-NONE -- one directed walk.

*Objective*: The Key Manager ROM request stays known across an IC_RESET override of the Key Manager. `hw/top/sep_ip_integration.sv` connects that request to the `prim_rom` chip select, and `prim_rom` asserts `noXOnCsI` on it on every clock (`vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/upstream/hw/ip/prim_generic/rtl/prim_rom.sv`). The port is `km_jtag_rst_n`, the TDO-end port of the SEP slice (`doc/integrator/src/smu.adoc`, SEP slice). The walk follows the IC_RESET field table in `hw/ip/jtag/jtag_ptap/doc/architecture.adoc`: stage `reset_control=0` with `reset_enable=1`, apply `reset_enable=0`, then release control and disable the override in separate Update-DR operations.

*Test Coverage*: stage, apply and release of the `km_jtag_rst_n` override while the KM fetches from its ROM; the request through a window that starts at the first fetch after the release.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring-up with the KM held in software reset. TRST the TDR, select it, and require the shifted-out word to be the TDR reset value.
. Release the KM through `SW_RESET_N`. The smoke image stores its SRAM marker and spins, so every loop turn is a ROM fetch.
. Stage and apply the override, one Update-DR each. Release `reset_control` while the override remains enabled, then disable the override in a second Update-DR. The host samples the KM ROM request counter after each operation.
. After the release, count the cycles to the first ROM request, then run a 4000-cycle fetch window.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,6",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |What it proves
|CHK-TDR-SELECT
|After TRST the TDR shifts out its reset value (every `reset_enable` and `reset_control` at 1). That the TDR word reaches the Key Manager reset is shown by CHK-TDR-APPLY.
|CHK-KM-LIVE
|The KM left software reset: the ROM request count rises and KM SRAM word0 holds the smoke marker.
|CHK-TDR-STAGE
|`reset_control=0` with `reset_enable=1` does not stop the KM: the count still rises.
|CHK-TDR-APPLY
|`reset_enable=0`, `reset_control=0` holds the KM: the count does not move over 64 cycles.
|CHK-TDR-RELEASE
|Releasing `reset_control` while the override remains enabled restarts the KM: a ROM request comes within 64 cycles of that Update-DR. Returning `reset_enable` to 1 in a separate Update-DR does not stop the requests. The log gives the latency and both counter values.
|CHK-KM-ROM-WINDOW
|At least 64 ROM requests in the 4000-cycle window after the release. `noXOnCsI` grades the request on every cycle of it; a firing prints `Error:` and the run fails.
|===
*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes, and the VCS log has no `noXOnCsI` failure.

*Anti-vacuity*: `noXOnCsI` is live in the VCS `lsu_stub_all_live` model: a diagnostic run that forced the request to X for three cycles printed the assertion failure and the run graded FAIL while every cocotb checker passed. The cocotb checkers cannot see X; they only prove the override reached the KM and that the KM fetches through the graded window.

*Not claimed*: any four-state property on Verilator, which is two-state and compiles no RTL assertions (see Known Limitations, "RTL assertions are backend-dependent"). The other seven SEP-slice ports. The KM ROM image is always loaded on this bench; a request that is X because the ROM array holds X is a bench condition and is not graded here.

'''''

[[sep_km_key_policy_vault_test]]
===== `sep_km_key_policy_vault_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Key Manager vault

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`, `+km_rom_hex=km_rom_vault.parhex`

*Source Module*: `sep_km_key_policy_vault_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, KM path / golden

*Randomization*: RANDCFG — slot, SRAM lock region and the seal/free slot pair from the run seed; extent endpoints 0 and 63 and the lock/W1C sequence are directed.

*Objective*: The key/policy vault refuses what it should: a store to a locked SRAM region is dropped with the readback unchanged, the matching `SRAM_WRITE_LOCK_VIOLATION` bit sets, a store past the KPV register map raises `IRQ_STATUS` AXI_SLVERR only, and the status bit clears W1C. The seal walk is here too: the same erase must retire a sealed slot and free an unsealed one.

*Test Coverage*: Vault lock, seal, retire, erase, and SRAM write-lock on the KM CPU bus.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Dedicated `km_rom_vault` on the KM CPU bus, not `rom_main`.
. The seal walk lives here rather than on the command set because no mailbox command seals a slot: over that surface an erase always frees, so the retire path has no vehicle there.
. PicoRV32 is RV32E (`x0`–`x15` only).
. A legal slot in 0..63 takes `CTRL.lock_write`; a store past `KM_KPV_SIZE` (`0x13108`, still inside the 8 KB KPV window) raises AXI SLVERR and not DECERR; a write to a write-locked SRAM region (never region 0 or 15) is dropped and raises `SRAM_WRITE_LOCK_VIOLATION` plus `IRQ_STATUS.SRAM_WRITE_LOCK_ERR`, then both W1C-clear to 0.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-SLOT
|`CTRL.lock_write` sticks on slots 0, the seed slot, and 63.
|CHK-LOCKWR
|A key-data write to a write-locked slot raises AXI SLVERR. This is the LOCK path, not the decode path CHK-EXTENT grades: a legal key-entry address on a locked slot. The sticky error bits are cleared and confirmed clean first, so an earlier fault cannot satisfy it.
|CHK-LOCKUSE
|A key-data read of a `lock_use` slot raises SLVERR and returns zero data. Both halves are required: the refusal alone would not show the data path is also forced.
|CHK-EXTENT
|Store to `0x13108` sets `IRQ_STATUS` AXI_SLVERR and leaves AXI_DECERR clear. `hw/ip/key_manager/regs/key_manager.rdl` (memory-map summary): an unmapped offset inside a window answers SLVERR.
|CHK-DROP
|Write to the locked SRAM region is dropped (readback unchanged).
|CHK-VIOL
|`SRAM_WRITE_LOCK_VIOLATION` reads EXACTLY the locked region's bit. Compared whole rather than masked, so a decoder that set every region's bit fails.
|CHK-IRQ
|`IRQ_STATUS.SRAM_WRITE_LOCK_ERR` is set and no ROM/SRAM parity or ROM-write error is raised alongside it.
|CHK-W1C
|Violation and IRQ bits read back 0 after W1C.
|CHK-SEAL
|One `CTRL` write on the seed's seal slot sets `seal` and raises `lock_write` with it, and `lock_use` and `erase` read 0. So the `lock_use` that CHK-RETIRE requires is set by the erase, not by the seal.
|CHK-RETIRE
|Erasing that sealed slot leaves it retired: `lock_write` held and `lock_use` gained.
|CHK-RETSTICK
|A SECOND erase of the retired slot leaves it retired. Retirement is stated to hold until warm reset, so it is graded as a sticky property rather than a one-shot transition.
|CHK-FREE
|An unsealed slot is marked with `lock_write`, and the erase clears `CTRL` entirely, so the slot is reusable. The mark matters: the reset value is already clear, so without it a no-op erase would pass. The contrast is the point: the same erase, one sealed and one not.
|CHK-ERASEDATA
|The erase overwrites the stored key words, not just the CTRL bookkeeping. Two words are preloaded before the slot is marked; after the erase both differ from the preload AND from each other, so a fill that never ran or that is stuck at one value fails.
|CHK-IRQSET
|`IRQ_SET.DRBG_ERR_SET` raises `IRQ_STATUS.DRBG_ERR`, and W1C clears it. This grades the software-inject path and the status plumbing ONLY -- it is not coverage of the DRBG fault source, and must not be recorded as such.
|CHK-RANDCFG
|The ROM echoes an eight-bit fold of the WHOLE config word, so all four seeded operands are proven received. Echoing only the slot would let a ROM that ignored the region or either seal-walk slot produce an identical result word.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Aperture*: the vault is on the KM CPU bus, so the ROM evaluates these
contracts and the host reads one summary word from KM SRAM. That indirection is
real and is stated here rather than left implied: the host observes a result
word, not the individual registers. What keeps it from being a firmware
self-report is the seeded configuration -- the slot, region and slot pair are
chosen by the host, and the result word carries a fold of the whole config word
the ROM received, so a firmware that reported success unconditionally would
still have to have consumed every one of the host's operands.

'''''

==== Key Manager vault vehicles

[[sep_km_command_set_rand_test]]
===== `sep_km_command_set_rand_test`

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, real fuse-sense, `+esrc_noise_force`,
`+km_rom_hex=rom_main.rom.parhex`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Key Manager vault

*Source Module*: `sep_km_command_set_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, KM path / golden

*Objective*: the KM mailbox command set is this vehicle's surface — generate,
transfer, revoke-or-shred, illegal-to-error, version queries, OTP cold lock,
recoverable-fault ACK, and a directed ABR seed transfer. The sideload KATs prove
only that a consumer can consume a key; none of them exercises the command
interface that produces, moves and destroys one.

*Test Coverage*: Generate, transfer, revoke-or-shred, version, OTP lock, recoverable ACK, ABR dest, and illegal-to-error on the KM mailbox.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Stage a valid PROD OTP image and sense it for real: the KM firmware reads OTP
at boot. Park OTBN, KMAC and HMAC, leave AES released as the consume witness, so
a transferred key can only land where the test says it lands.
. Bring entropy up strict, then release the KM and wait for `RESP_KM_READY`.
. Query `CMD_HW_VER`, `CMD_ROM_VER` and `CMD_SRAM_VER`.
. Generate a key for AES twice; check the handle and the echoed argument fields.
. Load a known key with `DEST_VALID = AES | xfer_dest`, transfer it to AES, and
encrypt: the ciphertext is compared against an independent AES-256-ECB golden.
. Transfer the same handle to the seed-selected dest (`xfer_dest`) in a
dedicated mailbox beat. When that dest is not AES, release that engine for
the key-bus write (OTBN waits idle after release), then park it again.
Consume of HMAC / KMAC / OTBN stays on the sideload KATs.
. Load an 8-word palindromic seed and transfer it to dest ABR ML-DSA seed
(`0x10`). Every seed walks this cell. Consume of that seed is
`sep_km_abr_seed_sideload_test`.
. Transfer the same handle to an engine outside its permitted set.
. Shred the AES key, then load and transfer a second, distinct known key and
encrypt again.
. Revoke the generated handle, then transfer on it.
. Revoke the reserved null handle; walk seeded undefined command IDs; send a
defined command with the wrong payload length. Probe with `CMD_STAT` after each
refusal.
. Lock one identity OTP bit with `CMD_OTP_READ_LOCK_COLD`; refuse a reserved bit
and a zero-length payload.
. Pulse SEP mailbox `CTRL.FLUSH`, receive `RESP_RECOVERABLE_FAULT`, ACK it, and
prove `CMD_STAT` clears the latched bit. The following shred is the key command
that must be accepted after the ACK.

*Randomization*: RANDCFG. Generate / transfer / revoke-or-shred /
illegal-to-error walk every seed. The generated key size, the extra transfer
dest (one of HMAC / KMAC / AES / OTBN), the out-of-policy destination (outside
`AES | xfer_dest`), and the undefined command IDs come from the run seed. The
undefined IDs always include the five IDs immediately outside the defined
runs. One seed writes one classic dest cell plus the directed ABR cell; the
card does not claim all four classic dests in one invocation.

*Scope*: sideload KATs stay consume proofs; this vehicle owns the mailbox command
set. The mailbox PROTOCOL is its own entry: see
`sep_km_mailbox_protocol_rand_test`. The seal
walk is on `sep_km_key_policy_vault_test`, not here: no mailbox command seals a
slot, so over this surface an erase always frees.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK0
|`rom_main` boots on real entropy and announces `RESP_KM_READY`. This is the bench build with the boot and unrecoverable-fault SRAM wipes compiled out; see Known Limitations, "KM application ROM wipe paths".
|CHK-HW-VER
|`CMD_HW_VER` returns success and `RETURN_ARG` equals the KMCSR VERSION word 1.0.0.
|CHK-ROM-VER
|`CMD_ROM_VER` returns success and the packed ROM 1.1.0 word: patch `[7:0]`, minor `[15:8]`, major `[23:16]`, `[31:24]` zero, per the return-argument layout in `hw/ip/key_manager/doc/firmware.adoc` (`km-cmd-rom-ver`). The KM firmware spec does not state a release number, so 1.1.0 is the DV-owned expected value. The test holds it as a constant and does not read it from the KM firmware headers, because the ROM builds its reply from those headers.
|CHK-SRAM-VER
|`CMD_SRAM_VER` returns the failure code: this image is ROM-only.
|CHK-GEN
|`CMD_KEY_GENERATE` returns success and a non-null handle, and its return argument echoes the requested size and destination mask.
|CHK-SAMPLER
|`CMD_KEY_GENERATE` raises the observed EDN->KM beat count on the DRBG scoreboard `CHK5_km` stream. The KMCSR sampler counters are not SEP-reachable.
|CHK-UNIQ
|A second generate returns a different handle.
|CHK-XFER
|`CMD_KEY_TRANSFER` of a known loaded key succeeds and the AES ciphertext equals the independent golden.
|CHK-XFER-DEST
|A dedicated `CMD_KEY_TRANSFER` of that same handle to the seed-selected dest returns success and echoes the dest. Consume of HMAC / KMAC / OTBN is not claimed here.
|CHK-ABR-DEST
|A directed `CMD_KEY_LOAD` + `CMD_KEY_TRANSFER` of an 8-word palindromic seed to dest ABR ML-DSA seed returns success and echoes that dest. Every seed walks this cell. Consume is not claimed here.
|CHK-DEST
|A transfer to an engine outside the key's permitted set is refused with the failure code, not an argument-validation code. The refusal is attributable: the target engine is released from reset and waited to idle for that beat, so being parked is not the cause, and an unconditional control beat transfers the same handle to a destination inside its permitted set and requires success, so a handle that no longer exists -- which carries the same failure code -- is not the cause either.
|CHK-SHRED
|`CMD_ENGINE_SHRED` succeeds with its destination echoed, and the engine re-keys: a second distinct key transferred afterwards encrypts to its own golden and no longer to the first key's.
|CHK-REVOKE
|`CMD_KEY_REVOKE` succeeds with the handle echoed.
|CHK-CLOSED
|A transfer on the revoked handle is refused with the failure code: revoke fails closed.
|CHK-NULL
|Revoking the reserved null handle is refused with the invalid-argument code.
|CHK-ILLEGAL
|Every seeded undefined command ID returns the invalid-command code.
|CHK-LEN
|A defined command carrying the wrong payload length returns the invalid-length code.
|CHK-FRAME
|The three framing rejections, each with the value it carries: a corrupt header CRC-8 returns RC_HEADER_CRC with the CRC the KM computed, a wrong sequence number returns RC_CMD_NOSEQ with the number it expected, and a corrupt payload CRC-32C returns RC_PAYLOAD_CRC. The sequence-counter side effects differ between the three and are handled in the driver.
|CHK-OTP
|`CMD_OTP_READ_LOCK_COLD` of one identity bit returns success with that bit set in the echo; a reserved bit returns the invalid-argument code; a zero-length payload returns the invalid-length code.
|CHK-RECOV
|SEP `CTRL.FLUSH` posts `RESP_RECOVERABLE_FAULT` with the flushed-by-SEP code; `CMD_STAT` reports the latched bit; aggregator bit 31 is 1; `CMD_RECOV_ACK` clears the STAT bit and the aggregator bit. The following shred is the key command accepted after the ACK.
|CHK-GONE
|A shredded engine refuses to start. After a final `CMD_ENGINE_SHRED` the AES produces no output within a bounded window, which is the only frontdoor evidence the shred reached the key: the engine key registers are write-only. Runs last, because an engine parked waiting for a key stays parked.
|CHK-ALIVE
|After every refusal `CMD_STAT` succeeds and reports no latched recoverable error. The second half is what gives CHK-DEST and CHK-CLOSED their meaning: while a recoverable fault is pending the KM refuses every key command with the same failure code those two expect, before it looks at the arguments at all, so a faulted KM would otherwise satisfy both without the policy they name being exercised.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Anti-vacuity*: every frame is built with a correct header CRC-8, a correct
declared length and a correct payload CRC-32C, so a refusal is a verdict on the
command ID or its arguments and never on framing. The refusal checkers assert
the exact documented code rather than "not success", so a rejection for the
wrong reason does not read as a pass. The two known keys are checked to encrypt
the plaintext differently before either is used, so CHK-SHRED's
before-and-after comparison cannot be satisfied by two identical goldens.

*Not claimed*: the DRBG fault SOURCE. The organic timeout and AXI-Stream error
conditions cannot be provoked from the SEP fabric, and at SEP level such a fault
is unrecoverable -- the KM emits an unsolicited fault response -- so it
is neither a readable status nor a return code from here. The interrupt plumbing
for that same bit IS reachable and is graded on the vault vehicle's CHK-IRQSET,
via the architected `IRQ_SET.DRBG_ERR_SET` inject; the two must not be conflated,
and CHK-IRQSET is not coverage of the source.

The mailbox is a transport here, not the subject. Its register surface -- depth,
full, the four overflow/underflow stickies, `IRQ_STATUS`, and `SEP_CTRL`'s
response modes and flush -- is owned by `sep_km_mailbox_protocol_rand_test`.

'''''

[[sep_km_wipe_reset_test]]
===== `sep_km_wipe_reset_test`

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, real fuse-sense, `+esrc_noise_force`,
`+km_rom_hex=rom_main.rom.parhex`

*Phase*: 1

*Bucket*: Key Manager vault

*Source Module*: `sep_km_wipe_reset_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: recover the KM after a `SW_RESET_N` warm pulse, then close it
with the SEP emergency wipe.

*Test Coverage*: KM warm-reset + `RESP_KM_READY`; `KM_WIPE_CTRL` unrecoverable fault.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Stage a valid PROD OTP image, bring entropy up, release the KM, wait for
`RESP_KM_READY`, and load a known key.
. Park then release the KM `SW_RESET_N` bit. Resynchronize mailbox sequence
counters. Wait for `RESP_KM_READY` and load the same known key again.
. Write `KM_WIPE_CTRL.wipe_state`. Receive `RESP_UNRECOVERABLE_FAULT`.

*Randomization*: RAND-NONE.

*Scope*: KPV-zero after wipe is not claimed -- those arrays have no SEP
frontdoor.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK0
|`rom_main` boots on real entropy and announces `RESP_KM_READY`. This is the bench build with the boot and unrecoverable-fault SRAM wipes compiled out; see Known Limitations, "KM application ROM wipe paths".
|CHK-PRE
|`CMD_KEY_LOAD` succeeds before the warm pulse.
|CHK-RESET
|After park/release the KM announces `RESP_KM_READY` and `CMD_KEY_LOAD` succeeds.
|CHK-WIPE
|A write of `KM_WIPE_CTRL.wipe_state` posts `RESP_UNRECOVERABLE_FAULT` with a one-word payload equal to the `wipe_state` fault code (-1, sign-extended to `0xFFFFFFFF`) from the Fault Codes table in `hw/ip/key_manager/doc/firmware.adoc`, and aggregator bit 30 (PIC source 31) is 1. A fault from any other cause carries a different code and fails.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Anti-vacuity*: the post-reset load is a value-bearing command, not only the
ready banner. Wipe is last: after the fault frame the test does not issue
another mailbox command.

'''''

[[sep_km_handover_test]]
===== `sep_km_handover_test`

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, real fuse-sense, `+esrc_noise_force`,
`+km_rom_hex=rom_main.rom.parhex`

*Phase*: 1

*Bucket*: Key Manager vault

*Source Module*: `sep_km_handover_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: the handover mailbox contract, a SRAM load, and `CMD_SRAM_EXEC` after warm reset.

*Test Coverage*: `CMD_EXEC_ROM` inhibit; `CMD_SRAM_LOAD_EXEC` 14-word stream; `CMD_SRAM_EXEC` after warm reset.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Stage a valid PROD OTP image, bring entropy up, release the KM, wait for
`RESP_KM_READY`.
. Refuse empty `CMD_SRAM_EXEC`, `CMD_SRAM_LOAD_EXEC` with `FW_WORDS=0`, and
`CMD_SRAM_LOAD_EXEC` with reserved bits set.
. `CMD_EXEC_ROM` succeeds once; a later `CMD_SRAM_LOAD_EXEC`, `CMD_SRAM_EXEC`
and `CMD_EXEC_ROM` each return the failure code. A warm reset clears the
inhibit.
. On a fresh epoch, send `CMD_SRAM_LOAD_EXEC` with `FW_WORDS=14`, receive
success, then stream the 14-word blob plus CRC-32C.
. Warm-reset the KM. `CMD_SRAM_EXEC` returns success.

*Randomization*: RAND-NONE.

*Scope*: KMCSR `TEST_SIGNATURE` is not SEP-reachable.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK0
|`rom_main` boots on real entropy and announces `RESP_KM_READY`. This is the bench build with the boot and unrecoverable-fault SRAM wipes compiled out; see Known Limitations, "KM application ROM wipe paths".
|CHK-EMPTY
|`CMD_SRAM_EXEC` with no image returns the invalid-argument code.
|CHK-ARG
|`CMD_SRAM_LOAD_EXEC` with `FW_WORDS=0` or reserved `[31:16]` set returns the invalid-argument code.
|CHK-INHIBIT
|The first `CMD_EXEC_ROM` succeeds; later handover commands return the failure code.
|CHK-INHIBIT-RST
|A warm reset clears the inhibit; `CMD_EXEC_ROM` succeeds again.
|CHK-LOAD
|`CMD_SRAM_LOAD_EXEC` returns success before the stream; the 14-word image plus CRC-32C is posted.
|CHK-EXEC
|After a warm reset `CMD_SRAM_EXEC` returns success.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Anti-vacuity*: inhibit cells assert the failure code, not "not success". The
load response is taken before the stream, matching the ROM handler. CHK-EMPTY
refuses `CMD_SRAM_EXEC` with no image, so CHK-EXEC success is against that
refusal, not an always-success command.

'''''

[[sep_km_abr_seed_sideload_test]]
===== `sep_km_abr_seed_sideload_test`

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, real fuse-sense, `+esrc_noise_force`,
`+km_rom_hex=rom_main.rom.parhex`

*Phase*: 1

*Bucket*: Key Manager vault

*Source Module*: `sep_km_abr_seed_sideload_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: a KM-sideloaded ABR ML-DSA seed produces the same public key as
a direct software-seed KEYGEN.

*Test Coverage*: KM dest ABR ML-DSA seed consume through the KV seed-read control.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
