. Stage a valid PROD OTP image, bring entropy up, release the KM, wait for
`RESP_KM_READY`.
. Run a direct-seed ML-DSA-87 KEYGEN on the palindromic seed with
`cfg.entropy` and capture the public key.
. `ZEROIZE`. Repeat KEYGEN with the inverted entropy vector. The two public
keys must match -- `ABR_ENTROPY` is SCA masking and must not change the
outputs.
. `ZEROIZE`, then `CMD_KEY_LOAD` + `CMD_KEY_TRANSFER` dest ABR ML-DSA seed.
. Write the same masking entropy, set the KV seed-read enable, KEYGEN, and
compare the public key word-for-word.

*Randomization*: RANDCFG. `SepAbrKeygenCfg` is the single source of truth for
masking entropy on both compare KEYGENs. The seed words are directed so the
two KEYGENs stay comparable.

*Scope*: ML-DSA only. The ML-KEM dest bits and the `CMD_ABR_SK_TRANSFER`
consume are claimed by `sep_km_abr_mlkem_sideload_test`.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK0
|`rom_main` boots on real entropy and announces `RESP_KM_READY`. This is the bench build with the boot and unrecoverable-fault SRAM wipes compiled out; see Known Limitations, "KM application ROM wipe paths".
|CHK-RANDCFG
|A KEYGEN that writes `cfg.entropy` and a KEYGEN that writes the inverted vector produce the same public key. Both compare KEYGENs write `cfg.entropy`.
|CHK-DIRECT
|The direct-seed KEYGEN produces a non-zero public key.
|CHK-XFER
|`CMD_KEY_TRANSFER` dest ABR ML-DSA seed returns success and echoes the dest.
|CHK-PK
|The KM-sideloaded KEYGEN public key equals the direct-seed public key.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Anti-vacuity*: both compare public keys must be non-zero. The inverted
vector is not equal to `cfg.entropy`. The palindrome absorbs the KV dword
reversal, so a CHK-PK mismatch is a sideload failure, not an ordering
artifact. `ABR_ENTROPY` is write-only; this row grades the RDL "no change on
the outputs" rule, not a consume probe.
'''''

[[sep_km_abr_mlkem_sideload_test]]
===== `sep_km_abr_mlkem_sideload_test`

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, real fuse-sense, `+esrc_noise_force`,
`+km_rom_hex=rom_main.rom.parhex`

*Phase*: 1

*Bucket*: Key Manager vault

*Source Module*: `sep_km_abr_mlkem_sideload_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: every ML-KEM lane of the Key-Vault facade carries what the KM
pushed into the sideload CSR, and the shared key Adams Bridge posts back over
the write lane is consumable by the KM.

*Test Coverage*: the 16-dword seed read lane carrying D then Z, the message
read lane, and the shared-key write lane with its `KEY_VALID` latch and its
interrupt, plus the `CMD_ABR_SK_TRANSFER` refusal when no key has been posted
and the vault-confidentiality rule that withholds the shared key from software.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Stage a valid PROD OTP image, bring entropy up, release the KM, wait for
`RESP_KM_READY`. Read `MLKEM_NAME` and log it as information only: `abr_reg.rdl`
gives it no reset and no SEP document gives its value, so it is not graded. A
dead decode reads both reference keys as zero and fails `CHK-KEM-SEED-REF`.
. KEYGEN on the ALT `(D, Z)` pair written directly to the registers and keep
the encapsulation key. `ZEROIZE`, then KEYGEN on the REF pair and require a
different encapsulation key. The registers are left holding REF.
. `ZEROIZE`. `CMD_KEY_LOAD` + `CMD_KEY_TRANSFER` the ALT `D` to dest
`abr_mlkem_seed_d` and the ALT `Z` to dest `abr_mlkem_seed_z`, set the KV
seed-read enable, and KEYGEN with no register write. The encapsulation key
must equal the ALT one and differ from the REF one.
. ENCAPS twice over that key, once on each message, written directly, and
require the two ciphertexts to differ. The first of the two also establishes
that the shared key is readable when nothing came from the vault. The register
is left holding REF.
. `ZEROIZE`, sideload the ALT message to dest `abr_mlkem_msg`, set the KV
message-read enable, and ENCAPS. The ciphertext must equal the ALT one, and
the shared key of that same ENCAPS must now read zero.
. With nothing yet posted on the write lane, `CMD_ABR_SK_TRANSFER` must be
refused. Then set the KV shared-key write enable and ENCAPS again. The KM must
post the unsolicited `RESP_ABR_SHARED_KEY_READY` announcement, and the same
command must then succeed with a non-zero KPV handle.

*Randomization*: none. Every seed, message and command is directed: each leg
is a value-equality compare between a sideloaded and a direct run, and a
random input on either side would remove the compare.

*Scope*: the KV `read_entry` / `write_entry` and `dest_valid` fields are not
claimed -- SEP has no Caliptra Key Vault, so the facade serves one block per
lane and ignores entry selection. The read-error response a block raises while
its `KEY_VALID` is clear is not claimed here.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK0
|`rom_main` boots on real entropy and announces `RESP_KM_READY`. This is the bench build with the boot and unrecoverable-fault SRAM wipes compiled out; see Known Limitations, "KM application ROM wipe paths".
|CHK-KEM-SEED-REF
|The ALT and REF `(D, Z)` pairs produce different encapsulation keys. Without this the sideload compare could not distinguish a delivered seed from a stale one.
|CHK-KEM-SEED-SIDELOAD
|A KEYGEN whose seed arrives only over the KV seed lane produces the encapsulation key of the direct-register KEYGEN on the same words, and not the key of the pair the registers last held. This is the only check that the lane's two blocks land in the right halves -- D on the low eight dwords and Z on the high eight.
|CHK-KEM-MSG-SIDELOAD
|An ENCAPS whose message arrives only over the KV message lane produces the ciphertext of the direct-register ENCAPS on the same message, and not that of the message the register last held. The compare is on the ciphertext, not the shared key: the ciphertext is public and a deterministic function of the encapsulation key and the message, while the shared key is not readable on exactly this path -- see the next row.
|CHK-KEM-SK-CONFIDENTIAL
|The shared key reads back over the bus after a fully register-driven ENCAPS, and reads zero once the message came from the vault. The ML-KEM shared key is consumed directly by the Key Manager and shared-key storage is secret-bearing Class 2 logic (`doc/crypto.adoc`, `doc/attack_countermeasures.adoc`), so a key the vault sourced must not be readable by software over the CSR aperture. The register-driven read is the positive control: without it a broken readback path would pass the zero check, and it is what makes this a conditional withholding rather than a readback that never worked.
|CHK-KEM-SK-NOTIFY
|The writeback raises the shared-key interrupt and the KM firmware posts the unsolicited `RESP_ABR_SHARED_KEY_READY` frame. The facade pulses `IRQ_STATUS.key_valid` on the final writeback dword and the wrapper gates it onto the shared-key interrupt, so this frame is the only host-side view of that path -- the consume below could succeed with the interrupt dead.
|CHK-KEM-SK-WRITEBACK
|`CMD_ABR_SK_TRANSFER` is refused while the sideload block's `KEY_VALID` is clear, and succeeds with a non-zero handle after an ENCAPS with the write lane armed. The refusal is the negative control: without it the success would not show that the writeback set `KEY_VALID`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Anti-vacuity*: every captured encapsulation key and ciphertext is required to
be non-zero. The shared key read on the register-driven path is required to be
non-zero, and it precedes the check that the vault-sourced shared key reads
zero. Each sideload leg is preceded by a run on different words, so the
register the lane bypasses holds the wrong value when the lane is read -- a
lane that delivers nothing fails on the inequality before the equality is
reached. The ML-KEM identity words are logged as information only. Seeds and messages are palindromes, so a
mismatch is a delivery failure and not the KM-versus-register dword ordering; D
and Z are *different* palindromes, so a swap between them still fails. The
shared-key consume carries its own refusal leg rather than resting on a single
success.

'''''

[[sep_km_mailbox_protocol_rand_test]]
===== `sep_km_mailbox_protocol_rand_test`

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`, KM held in `SW_RESET_N` (reset default).
No `+km_rom_hex`: this leaf grades the SEP-side registers, not a command stream.

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Key Manager vault

*Source Module*: `sep_km_mailbox_protocol_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Probe*: `km_mbox_sep_wr_err_count_o` / `km_mbox_sep_wr_err_addr_o` (`tb/tb_top.sv`) count the write refusals of the SEP-side mailbox regblock decode and hold the last refused offset. Observation-only: they read DUT nets and drive nothing. Signed off by yenhenglai on 2026-09-29.

*Objective*: the SEP-side KM mailbox as a REGISTER surface rather than a
transport. `STATUS` depth and full/empty, the two SEP-reachable sticky error
bits, `IRQ_STATUS` / `IRQ_ENABLE` gating onto aggregator `[14]`, both
`SEP_CTRL` response modes, and flush.

*Test Coverage*: SEP-side KM mailbox registers: depth, stickies, IRQ, response modes, flush.

*Why it is its own entry*: a transport only ever touches what a well-formed
exchange needs. `sep_km_command_set_rand_test` reaches 4 of the 7 SEP-side
registers and 2 of the 12 `STATUS` bits. Reaching the rest means reading an
empty FIFO and overrunning a full one, and both corrupt a frame in flight.

*Why KM is held*: the SEP-facing mailbox lives on cold reset, so the FIFOs and
SEP registers stay alive while the KM CPU cannot consume inbound words or push
outbound ones. Fill, overflow and underflow are then deterministic, and
firmware cannot flush or fault the mailbox under the host. A `SEP_CTRL` write
after a deliberate underflow is a register-block access, independent of the
FIFO error path, and a 32-bit beat must stay OKAY. A 64-bit beat at
`SEP_CTRL` (offset 0x18) reaches past the register file, and its single write
response must be SLVERR or DECERR. One `BRESP` covers the whole beat, so it
cannot say which half, or which block, refused it. The crypto demux routes the
beat on its start address, so it reaches the Key Manager port; the mailbox
register block's own address decode (PeakRDL `--err-if-bad-addr`) is then
observed through the tb_top probe `km_mbox_sep_wr_err_count_o` /
`km_mbox_sep_wr_err_addr_o`, and it must refuse exactly the upper word, at the
first offset past the RDL window. The `SEP_CTRL` value after the beat is
logged and not graded (known limitation _Dead-space per-beat refusal on
writes_). Negative accesses are marked `expect_error` so the
environment scoreboard does not fail them as real errors.

*Randomization*: RANDCFG. The 16 inbound fill words and the overflow poke come
from the run seed. Every discrete cell (idle, underflow SLVERR, underflow
OKAY-mode, fill, overflow SLVERR, overflow OKAY-mode, IRQ enable/gate/W1C,
flush) walks in one invocation.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up no_cpu with fuse sense skipped. Require `SW_RESET_N` bit 0 clear.
. Compare reset `STATUS` and `IRQ_STATUS` against the empty golden.
. Read empty `SEP_READ_DATA`; require SLVERR, data 0, and `outbound_underflow`
  latched in both `STATUS` and `IRQ_STATUS`.
. Write `SEP_CTRL` with a 32-bit beat immediately after that underflow;
  require OKAY and an exact readback. Then write `SEP_CTRL` with one 64-bit
  beat whose low word sets one more response-mode bit; require SLVERR or
  DECERR with no timeout and log the readback. Require the mailbox register
  block's write-refusal count to step by exactly one across the beat, at
  offset `KM_MAILBOX_SEP_REG_MAP_SIZE`, and not to step across the 32-bit
  writes. Rewrite `SEP_CTRL` with the 32-bit value and require an exact
  readback.
. Set `outbound_underflow_resp`; the same empty read must be OKAY with the
  stickies still set. Write-1-to-clear both copies.
. Write 16 seeded words; after each write, `STATUS` depth/full/empty and
  `IRQ_STATUS.inbound_write_space_avail` must match the golden.
. A 17th write must SLVERR, latch `inbound_overflow`, and leave depth 16.
. Set `inbound_overflow_resp`; the next overrun must be OKAY, drop the word,
  and leave the sticky set.
. Enable the two error IRQs; aggregator `[14]` must rise. Clear the enable;
  the pin must drop while `IRQ_STATUS` stays. Write-1-to-clear both stickies.
. Latch inbound overflow (write to the full FIFO) and outbound underflow (read
  the empty FIFO). Set flush. The inbound FIFO empties, the outbound FIFO stays
  empty, `flush` self-clears, and both stickies survive in STATUS and
  `IRQ_STATUS` until write-1-to-clear.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-HELD
|`SW_RESET_N` bit 0 reads 0. Without this the fill/overflow goldens are a race against firmware.
|CHK-IDLE
|Reset `STATUS` and `IRQ_STATUS` match the empty golden; aggregator `[14]` is 0.
|CHK-UFL
|Empty `SEP_READ_DATA` returns SLVERR and 0, and `outbound_underflow` latches in both `STATUS` and `IRQ_STATUS`.
|CHK-CTRL
|A 32-bit `SEP_CTRL` write immediately after that underflow is OKAY and reads back exactly. The register is not the FIFO error path. A 64-bit write beat at offset 0x18 reaches past the register file and returns SLVERR or DECERR with no timeout. One `BRESP` covers the beat, so which half was refused is not graded; the readback after it is logged. The 32-bit write to the same register is the positive control.
|CHK-CTRL-EP
|The mailbox register block's own address decode refuses the upper word of that 64-bit beat: its write-refusal count steps by exactly one during the beat, and the refused block offset is the first offset past the RDL window (`KM_MAILBOX_SEP_REG_MAP_SIZE`, the word after `SEP_CTRL`). The 32-bit `SEP_CTRL` writes before and after the beat do not step the count, so the probe does not count legal writes. This shows the `--err-if-bad-addr` check is reachable from the SEP host; the beat's `BRESP` is logged.
|CHK-UFL-RESP
|`SEP_CTRL.outbound_underflow_resp` turns the same empty read into OKAY; the sticky bits stay set.
|CHK-UFL-W1C
|Write-1-to-clear drops both sticky copies.
|CHK-FILL
|16 seeded writes fill inbound. After each write, `STATUS` depth/full/empty and `IRQ_STATUS.inbound_write_space_avail` match the golden.
|CHK-OVR
|A 17th write returns SLVERR, latches `inbound_overflow`, and leaves depth 16.
|CHK-OVR-RESP
|`SEP_CTRL.inbound_overflow_resp` turns the next overrun into OKAY; the sticky bit stays; depth stays 16 (the word is dropped).
|CHK-IRQ
|With the `inbound_overflow` sticky set, enabling the two error IRQs raises aggregator `[14]`. Clearing the enable drops the pin while `IRQ_STATUS` stays unchanged, and setting it again raises the pin. With the enable still set, a write-1-to-clear of the two sticky error bits in `STATUS` and then in `IRQ_STATUS` drops the pin. `STATUS` and `IRQ_STATUS` then match the golden with both stickies clear and inbound depth 16; `CHK-IRQ-W1C` labels a failure of that compare.
|CHK-FLUSH
|`CTRL.FLUSH` empties the full inbound FIFO, self-clears, and leaves both sticky errors -- `inbound_overflow` and `outbound_underflow`, each latched just before the flush -- set in STATUS and `IRQ_STATUS` until W1C. The outbound FIFO is empty before and after, so its flush is not claimed; see Known Limitations, "KM mailbox outbound flush".
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Not claimed*: `inbound_underflow`, `outbound_overflow`, and
`flushed_by_km` need the KM CPU as the peer. `STATUS.inbound_separator`
updates only when the KM pops the inbound FIFO, and `STATUS.outbound_separator`
only when the SEP pops a KM-pushed word, so both stay on the KM IP mailbox
vehicles. `outbound_read_data_avail` stays 0 for the same reason.

'''''

[[sep_km_crc_pcpi_kat_test]]
===== `sep_km_crc_pcpi_kat_test`

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`, `+km_rom_hex=km_rom_crc.parhex`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Key Manager vault

*Source Module*: `sep_km_crc_pcpi_kat_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, KM path / golden

*Objective*: each mode of the KM CRC engine produces the value an independently
written host-side model produces. The engine has no register interface, so the
proof has to come from KM firmware issuing the PCPI custom-0 instruction.

*Test Coverage*: KM CRC PCPI modes vs an independent host golden.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Release the KM on a dedicated service ROM. The ROM applies one CRC instruction
per request — mode, chaining state, data operand — and returns the raw result.
It decides nothing.
. Walk the seeded operand set through all three modes and compare each result
against the host-side golden.
. Send one word through the word instruction and the same four bytes through
four chained byte instructions.
. Send one state and byte through both polynomial families.
. Repeat each byte-mode operand with garbage in the upper three bytes.

*Randomization*: RANDCFG — the vector set, from the run seed.

*Aperture*: the CRC engine has no register interface at all. It is a PicoRV32
PCPI co-processor reached by a custom-0 instruction, so the only honest
activation is KM firmware, in the ROM pattern the vault test established. The
assembler has no mnemonic for the instruction, so the image emits encoded words;
the KM core is built without the upper register file, so the encodings target
two registers in the lower half and cannot name one the core does not have.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-WORD
|Every seeded CRC-32C word vector equals the independent golden.
|CHK-BYTE
|Every seeded CRC-32C byte vector equals the independent golden.
|CHK-ROHC
|Every seeded CRC-8/ROHC vector equals the independent golden.
|CHK-XCHAIN
|One word instruction equals four chained byte instructions from the same start state. Hardware on both sides, so it holds independently of the golden.
|CHK-POLY
|The two polynomial families return different results for the same state and byte, compared over the low byte so their differing result widths cannot satisfy it. The operand is screened for this: the two agree in the low byte for roughly one input in 256, which is correct hardware, so it is excluded from the draw rather than tolerated by a looser check.
|CHK-NARROW
|CRC-8/ROHC leaves the upper twenty-four result bits clear.
|CHK-UPPER
|Both byte modes ignore the operand's upper twenty-four bits.
|CHK-MOVE
|No result equals its own input state.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Anti-vacuity*: the golden is anchored to published check values for both
algorithms and to a byte-order self-test, all asserted at import, so a
transcription error fails before a run rather than agreeing with an equally
wrong DUT. The operand draw rejects an all-zero state with an all-zero byte,
which every reflected CRC maps to zero and which therefore passes against a dead
engine; words whose four bytes are not all distinct, which cannot separate
little-endian consumption from big-endian; and operands sitting on a fixed point
of their mode, which the engine correctly maps back onto their own input state.
A fixed point is legal hardware behaviour, not a defect -- in CRC-8/ROHC one
byte in 256 is a fixed point for any given state -- so it is screened out of the
draw with the independent golden rather than tolerated by CHK-MOVE, which would
otherwise be unfalsifiable for that vector. CHK-MOVE then fails a co-processor
that hands back its first operand, and CHK-XCHAIN and CHK-POLY need no golden at
all. All three modes walk in this vehicle; do not split them into siblings.

*Not claimed*: the illegal-encoding trap. An unrecognised instruction in this
family produces no handshake and traps the core, which would end the service
loop; the KM firmware's own regression owns that leg.

'''''

[[sep_km_kpv_scrambler_test]]
===== `sep_km_kpv_scrambler_test`

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`,
`+km_rom_hex=km_rom_kpv_scrambler.parhex`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Key Manager vault

*Source Module*: `sep_km_kpv_scrambler_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, KM path / golden

*Objective*: the key-policy vault scrambler transforms both the stored word and
the address mapping while enabled. Pass-through while disabled is the
observation aperture this test relies on rather than a contract it asserts --
it is what makes the raw stored words readable frontdoor, and a vault that did
not pass through would fail the round-trip and the count instead.

*Test Coverage*: Vault scrambler enable, key transform, and sticky lock.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Release the KM on a dedicated observation ROM and post two scrambler keys and
three logical key-entry indices.
. With the scrambler off, zero all 1024 key-entry words. That reaches a known
starting state through a legitimate frontdoor, without a wipe and without
relying on power-up values.
. Program the first key, enable the scrambler, write one plaintext at each of
the three logical indices, then disable and scan the whole file.
. Repeat with the second key at the first index only.
. Re-enable and read the first index back.
. Take the sticky lock last, then read the key register and the first index
again.

*Randomization*: RANDCFG — two scrambler keys and three logical indices, drawn
distinct, from the run seed.

*Aperture*: the vault is reachable only from the KM side, so this needs its own
ROM image, not `rom_main` — that image programs the key, enables the scrambler
and takes the sticky lock during boot, so the enable toggle is already gone
before the host can act. No hierarchical peek is needed: with the scrambler
disabled the vault addresses its register file with the logical index and passes
data through unmodified, so firmware can write plaintext with scrambling on,
disable it, and read the raw stored words back through a legitimate frontdoor.
The lock is taken last because it is sticky. The ROM reports raw observations
and the host holds every verdict.

*Precondition*: `km_kpv_regfile` has no reset, so power-up is undefined and a
whole-vault "exactly N non-zero" scan is not a contract until the file is
known-zero. The zero fill is *setup*, not a wipe security checker.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-COUNT
|After three writes through the enabled scrambler, exactly three of the 1024 words differ from the zeroed setup.
|CHK-DATA
|No stored word equals any plaintext written.
|CHK-ADDR
|The stored words do not all sit at their own logical index: the address mapping is tweaked, not identity.
|CHK-KEYED
|The same plaintext at the same logical index under a different key stores a different word, so the transform is keyed rather than a fixed obfuscation. The reported index is range-checked and the value required non-zero first, so a second-key pass whose write never landed fails here instead of being read as evidence of keying.
|CHK-RT
|With the scrambler re-enabled, the logical index reads back the exact plaintext.
|CHK-KEYRD
|Before the lock, the key register reads back the key that was written. This is the control for CHK-SWWEL: without it a write-only register would make a later refused write look like a lock.
|CHK-LOCKRT
|The round-trip still recovers the plaintext after the lock, so the hardware kept the key it was using.
|CHK-SWWEL
|The lock's documented contract: after it, a write of a different key and a write clearing ENABLE are both refused -- the round-trip still returns the plaintext and ENABLE still reads set.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Anti-vacuity*: "exactly three" is the load-bearing quantifier, not "at least
one". A write that never landed gives zero differences and fails; a read path
stuck at zero gives zero and fails; a register file left dirty gives more than
three and fails. A bare "the stored word differs from the plaintext" check would
pass in the first two cases, and under a simulator that defaults uninitialised
memory to zero it would also pass on a file that was never written. Round-trip
is a required conjunct, so a scrambler that destroys data cannot pass by looking
scrambled. The plaintext is chosen with mixed nibbles because the substitution
is nibble-wise and a uniform value would weaken the difference signal.

CHK-ADDR is "not all of them" rather than "each of them". The
address transform is a bijection on the 1024 indices, and a bijection may have
fixed points, so a single index mapping to itself is legal; an identity mapping
across three seeded indices is not.

The architecture document requires only that a locked key cannot be modified.
CHK-SWWEL grades that contract. The register readback after lock is not claimed.

*Not claimed*: the post-lock key-register data value, the eraser's use of the
shared scrambler, the seal and retire contracts owned by the vault test, and
the wipe security contract.

'''''

[[sep_km_rom_write_err_test]]
===== `sep_km_rom_write_err_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Key Manager vault

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`, `+km_rom_hex=km_rom_bus_err.parhex`

*Source Module*: `sep_km_rom_write_err_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, KM CPU image

*Tracking*: #2494

*Randomization*: RAND-NONE — one directed store.

*Objective*: `hw/ip/key_manager/regs/key_manager.rdl` (memory-map Access Notes) says the KM ROM is read-only and is accessed through the PicoRV32 native memory interface, not AXI-Lite. A write completes with no error response, leaves the ROM unchanged and sets `IRQ_STATUS.ROM_WRITE_ERR`. The KM CPU observes an AXI error response only as the sticky `IRQ_STATUS.AXI_SLVERR` / `AXI_DECERR` bits (`km_csr.rdl`), so a ROM write leaves both clear.

*Test Coverage*: KM ROM write refusal, ROM content after a write, and the error the KM CPU observes.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Dedicated `km_rom_bus_err` on the KM CPU bus, not `rom_main`. The image makes each access, copies raw words to KM SRAM and grades nothing; the host holds every verdict (`seq_lib/sep_km_bus_err_seq.py` holds the word layout).
. Before each access the image writes 1 to clear `AXI_SLVERR`, `AXI_DECERR` and `ROM_WRITE_ERR` and stores the readback. The host requires it clear, so a bit a row reports belongs to that row's access. After each access the image polls `IRQ_STATUS` a bounded number of times and stores the last value.
. Control: a store to `KM_KPV_SIZE` past the KPV base, inside the 8 KB KPV window.
. The image loads a ROM word, stores its complement to it, polls, and loads the word again.
. The host waits for the image's marker in KM SRAM word 0 and reads the dump words through `km_sram_probe_o`.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-KM-BUS-ERR-LIVE
|Precondition, shared by the three `km_rom_bus_err` leaves (`seq_lib/sep_km_bus_err_seq.py`). KM SRAM word 0 holds the image's pre-load or post-load marker within a fixed cycle bound after release. The image stores the pre-load marker after every dump word except the data and `IRQ_STATUS` words of the virtual ROM load, so the image ran and the rows before that load are written. The checker has no PASS line: when the marker does not appear, the leaf stops with this FAIL and the KM ROM fetch count.
|CHK-KM-SLVERR-LIVE
|Control. A store past the KPV register map sets `IRQ_STATUS.AXI_SLVERR`, so the bit and the image's poll work.
|CHK-KM-ROM-UNCHANGED
|The ROM word reads the image constant before the store and after a store of its complement.
|CHK-KM-ROM-WRITE-ERR
|The store sets `IRQ_STATUS.ROM_WRITE_ERR`.
|CHK-KM-ROM-WRITE-NO-AXI-ERR
|The store leaves `IRQ_STATUS.AXI_SLVERR` and `AXI_DECERR` clear: `key_manager.rdl` says a ROM write completes with no error response and is reported only as `ROM_WRITE_ERR`. `CHK-KM-SLVERR-LIVE` shows the `AXI_SLVERR` bit sets on an erroring store, so a clear bit here is evidence.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. `UVM_ERROR` is 0.

*Current status*: green. Every named checker PASSes.

'''''

[[sep_km_otp_shim_unreachable_test]]
===== `sep_km_otp_shim_unreachable_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Key Manager vault

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`, `+km_rom_hex=km_rom_bus_err.parhex`

*Source Module*: `sep_km_otp_shim_unreachable_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, KM CPU image

*Randomization*: RANDCFG — the value the host programs into `EFUSE_BANK_INIT_TIME`, from the run seed, distinct from its reset and from the value the KM image stores.

*Objective*: `hw/ip/key_manager/regs/key_manager.rdl` (OTP / eFuse Pass-Through) says only the MAP, CTRL and MMR register maps of the 4 KB OTP window are forwarded to SEP, that any other offset in the window answers SLVERR, and that `efuse_shim_ctrl` is intentionally excluded from the KM AXI-Lite path. A KM CPU load from, and a store to, `OTP_EFUSE_MMR` base + size is the first word past the last forwarded register map, so it must answer SLVERR and must not reach the shim register.

*Test Coverage*: KM OTP window decode past the MMR register map, and the separation of the KM path from the eFuse shim CSR port.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. The host reads `EFUSE_BANK_INIT_TIME` on its SEP-side window (`SEP_EXTERNAL_EFUSE_SHIM_CTRL`) and requires its RDL reset, writes the seeded value, and reads it back.
. The host releases the KM into `km_rom_bus_err` (see <<sep_km_rom_write_err_test>> for the clear-then-poll rule of each row).
. Control: a KM load from the Reserved ROM-growth row (ROM base + ROM size) of the `key_manager.rdl` memory-map summary.
. The image loads from, then stores a fixed value to, the first word past the MMR register map.
. Control: the image loads from the mapped `OTP_EFUSE_MMR` `SEC_DISABLE_TOKEN_MATCH` register in the same OTP window.
. The host reads `EFUSE_BANK_INIT_TIME` again on its SEP-side window.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-KM-BUS-ERR-LIVE
|Precondition, shared by the three `km_rom_bus_err` leaves (`seq_lib/sep_km_bus_err_seq.py`). KM SRAM word 0 holds the image's pre-load or post-load marker within a fixed cycle bound after release. The image stores the pre-load marker after every dump word except the data and `IRQ_STATUS` words of the virtual ROM load, so the image ran and the rows before that load are written. The checker has no PASS line: when the marker does not appear, the leaf stops with this FAIL and the KM ROM fetch count.
|CHK-KM-SHIM-HOST-PATH
|Control. The host read of `EFUSE_BANK_INIT_TIME` is OKAY at its RDL reset, and the seeded value the host writes reads back, so the no-alias rows below can fail.
|CHK-KM-DECERR-LIVE
|Control. A KM load from the Reserved ROM-growth row sets `IRQ_STATUS.AXI_DECERR`, so the bit and the image's poll work.
|CHK-KM-OTP-WINDOW-LIVE
|Control. A KM load from the mapped `OTP_EFUSE_MMR` `SEC_DISABLE_TOKEN_MATCH` register sets no AXI error bit, so the OTP window answers a mapped offset and a SLVERR below is about the offset past the MMR register map.
|CHK-KM-OTP-SHIM-EXCLUDED
|The KM load and the KM store each set `AXI_SLVERR` and not `AXI_DECERR`; the load does not return the host value; the shim register still holds the host value after the KM store. All four observations are logged before the verdict.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. `UVM_ERROR` is 0.

*Refusal code*: SLVERR. The Key Manager answers every offset of the 4 KB OTP/eFuse window (`0x0001_1000`–`0x0001_1FFF`) outside the MAP, CTRL and MMR register maps itself, with SLVERR and read data `0xBADCAB1E`.

'''''

[[sep_km_vrom_decerr_test]]
===== `sep_km_vrom_decerr_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Key Manager vault

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`, `+km_rom_hex=km_rom_bus_err.parhex`

*Source Module*: `sep_km_vrom_decerr_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, KM CPU image

*Randomization*: RAND-NONE — one directed load.

*Objective*: The memory-map summary in `hw/ip/key_manager/regs/key_manager.rdl` does not list the virtual ROM (VROM) window `0x1000_0000`–`0x1000_FFFF` and ends with "Every other address | Reserved/Unmapped | DECERR". The KM CPU wrapper (`hw/ip/key_manager/rtl/picorv32_wrapper.sv`) decodes the window only when the simulation-only `OCAH_KM_VROM` define is set. SEP DV does not set it, so the SEP build must send the range to the AXI crossbar like any other unmapped address. This leaf grades the RDL summary: a KM CPU load from `0x1000_0000` must complete and answer DECERR.

*Test Coverage*: KM CPU access to the VROM range, which the `key_manager.rdl` memory-map summary does not list and the SEP build does not decode.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. The host releases the KM into `km_rom_bus_err` (see <<sep_km_rom_write_err_test>> for the clear-then-poll rule of each row). The virtual ROM load is the image's last access.
. Control: a KM load from the Reserved ROM-growth row, as in <<sep_km_otp_shim_unreachable_test>>.
. The image stores a pre-load marker to KM SRAM word 0, loads from the virtual ROM base, stores the data and the polled `IRQ_STATUS`, then stores a post-load marker.
. The host waits for the post-load marker for a fixed bound after the pre-load marker.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-KM-BUS-ERR-LIVE
|Precondition, shared by the three `km_rom_bus_err` leaves (`seq_lib/sep_km_bus_err_seq.py`). KM SRAM word 0 holds the image's pre-load or post-load marker within a fixed cycle bound after release. The image stores the pre-load marker after every dump word except the data and `IRQ_STATUS` words of the virtual ROM load, so the image ran and the rows before that load are written. The checker has no PASS line: when the marker does not appear, the leaf stops with this FAIL and the KM ROM fetch count.
|CHK-KM-DECERR-LIVE
|Control. A KM load from the Reserved ROM-growth row sets `IRQ_STATUS.AXI_DECERR`, so an erroring load completes on this bus and the bit reports it.
|CHK-KM-VROM-PRE
|The image reached the virtual ROM load, with the watched `IRQ_STATUS` bits clear just before it.
|CHK-KM-VROM-DECERR
|The post-load marker appears within the bound, and the load set `IRQ_STATUS.AXI_DECERR`. The claim is completion, so expiry of the bound is a failure, graded against an image shown alive up to the load.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. `UVM_ERROR` is 0.

'''''

=== Regression Groups

`README.md` owns the group table and the commands, including which group to
run when closing a change. This file owns each leaf's checker contract.

[[known-limitations]]
[[phase-3]]
=== Known Limitations

This table holds two different kinds of item, and the distinction matters when
reading a row: a *hardware* gap or spec-vs-RTL divergence, where the RTL or the
specification is expected to change; and a *firmware* constraint, where the
hardware is correct as built and software must program it correctly. A firmware
constraint is not an open defect.

[cols="2,4",options="header"]
|===
|Topic |Contract

|Token-comparator collapse inject (`3'b001`)
|`tb_top` mode `3'b001` forces instance 0 to a collapsed pair and leaves instances 1 and 2 as a legal match. The fault output is the OR of collapse and disagree, so `CHK-COLLAPSE` does not prove the collapse detector exists. Isolating that term needs all three pairs alike and `compute_comparison_vld_i=1`. SEC_DISABLE's valid is the sticky digest valid, not the RMA `digest_vld && fuse_sense_done` term, so an all-alike force with SEC valid still 0 is expected not to raise that rail.

|Inbound CFG allow-list (firmware constraint)
|Hardware does not hard-block allow-listing the inbound-filter CFG window, outbound CFG, the remap banks, or `SEP_GLOBAL_BASE_ADDR` / `SEP_REGION_SIZE`, and is correct as built: the filter applies the rules it is given. Firmware must keep those CSRs outside every allow window — allow-listing them is a programming error, not a hardware defect, and nothing is expected to change in the RTL. The suite proves ownership under correct programming (`CHK-OWNERSHIP`).

|Routed-but-reserved spans (specification)
|`sep_axi_map_refuse_test` drives reserved addresses and accepts any refusal; it does not grade the code `sep.rdl` states for each. Named reserved spans are excluded from that walk with a counted reason. The system-bus reserved span (`0x10A4_0000`–`0x10A5_FFFF`) is asserted by `sep_unmapped_access_policy_test`, which grades refusal, the code the SEP memory map states, and no alias there.

|KM application ROM wipe paths
|Every leaf that boots `rom_main` (`sep_drbg_real_sink_multi_km_aes_test`, `sep_km_otbn_sideload_kat_test`, `sep_km_aes_sideload_kat_test`, `sep_km_hmac_sideload_kat_test`, `sep_km_kmac_sideload_kat_test`, `sep_km_command_set_rand_test`, `sep_km_wipe_reset_test`, `sep_km_handover_test`, `sep_km_abr_seed_sideload_test`, `sep_km_abr_mlkem_sideload_test`) boots the KM application ROM from `hw/ip/key_manager/approm/prod` built with `KM_BOOT_WIPE=0 KM_UNREC_WIPE=0` (`cocotb/tests/km_fw/Makefile`). The production default wipes KM SRAM at boot and on an unrecoverable fault; on this bench the boot wipe halts before `RESP_KM_READY`. The boot-time and fault-time SRAM shred of the shipped ROM is therefore not exercised by these leaves, and no claim here covers it.

|KM CPU bus error visibility
|The KM CPU has no bus-error input, so it observes an error response only as the sticky `IRQ_STATUS.AXI_SLVERR` / `AXI_DECERR` bits; `sep_km_key_policy_vault_test` CHK-EXTENT and CHK-LOCKUSE show those bits set on an erroring store and load while the image runs on.

|Locked filter entry write response (hardware)
|`hw/ip/axi_filter/regs/filter_ctrl.rdl` (`FILTER_CONFIG.locked`) and `hw/ip/axi_filter/doc/index.adoc` ("Locking a Filter Entry") say that a write to a locked entry's `FILTER_CONFIG`, `START_ADDR` or `END_ADDR` goes to the AXI error subordinate and completes DECERR. On SEP the write completes SLVERR, on the inbound and the outbound bank, and the word keeps its value. `CHK-WOSET` in `sep_fabric_remap_filter_csr_bank_test` and `CHK-CONFIG-LOCK` in `sep_fabric_inbound_filter_rule_matrix_test` grade DECERR and fail until the RTL or the specification changes. `CHK-CONFIG-LOCK` grades the response code after its 2-beat `allow_burst` read, so that read still runs on the current RTL.

|KM mailbox outbound flush
|`sep_km_mailbox_protocol_rand_test` holds the Key Manager in software reset so fill, overflow and underflow are deterministic. Only the KM CPU pushes into the outbound FIFO, so that FIFO is empty when `CTRL.FLUSH` runs, and CHK-FLUSH proves the inbound flush only. A flush of a non-empty outbound FIFO needs the KM running and is not claimed.

|Dead-space per-beat refusal on writes (AXI)
|Read bursts are proven per beat: the bus monitor keeps each beat's `RRESP` in order, so `CHK-DEADSPACE-BEAT` can name the beat that answered. Write bursts cannot be, and no tooling will change it — AXI returns one `BRESP` for a whole write burst, so a write burst that refused only its tail is indistinguishable from one that refused every beat. The write contract stays at burst granularity: the burst is refused and no live register moves. Where the refusing block's own decode is observed, the refusal is named per word: `sep_km_mailbox_protocol_rand_test` CHK-CTRL-EP shows the KM mailbox register block refuses the upper word of a 64-bit beat at `SEP_CTRL`, at the first offset past its window.

|Inbound global-to-local remap consume (outside this DUT)
|`fabric.adoc` names `SEP_GLOBAL_BASE_ADDR` and `SEP_REGION_SIZE` as the global SEP aperture "used by the SMU crossbar and by SEP inbound global-to-local remap", which reads as a datapath this suite could drive. It is not one here. Bare SEP exports both CSR values on output ports (`sep_global_base_addr_o`, `sep_region_size_o` in `hw/sys/sep/rtl/sep.sv`) and nothing inside the DUT consumes them to translate an address; the remapper is in the SMU integration logic above this level. Measured rather than assumed: with the reset aperture programmed, an external read at the aperture-relative address of a scratch CSR returns DECERR while the same register answers at its own address, so the external master presents SEP-local addresses and no in-DUT remap stands between them. The CSR read/write path is graded by `sep_fabric_remap_filter_csr_bank_test`; the consume belongs to whoever integrates SEP, and a leaf here could only re-prove the register. The sixteen programmable `axi_alias_remap` regions are a different mechanism and are graded by `sep_fabric_local_alias_datapath_test`.

|Entropy pool pointer fault, PIC source 39 (hardware not built)
|`sep_internal_interrupts[38]` is reserved for the entropy pool FIFO's pointer-integrity error. The check is not built in this configuration, so the source is tied to zero, can never assert, and no vehicle grades it. `interrupts.adoc` documents the same. A built check is level-sensitive with no write-one-to-clear, so firmware must mask the source or scrub the pool rather than acknowledge it.

|Shadow map before sense with SEC_DIS active (hardware)
|`sep_sec_dis_override_test` is red. With `sec_dis=1` and `sep_fuse_sense_done_o=0`, a read of the `LC_STATE` shadow-map word returns an error response instead of AXI OKAY, because the map gate input is tied off. `CHK-MAP-OPEN` fails, and every other checker of the leaf PASSes. The leaf stays red while that input is tied off.

|Write burst past a block extent (hardware)
|`sep_fabric_burst_wrap_test` is red. A write burst that starts on the last live word of `sep_reset_ctrl`, `wdt_timer` or `secure_dma` is routed on its first address, and a later beat past the extent lands on a live register: `SW_RESET_N` answers OKAY and changes; `WKUP_THOLD_HI` and `SRC_ADDR_LO` answer SLVERR and still change. `hw/sys/sep/doc/memory_map.adoc` says an access past the extent never reaches a unit. Do not XFAIL.

|Non-incrementing burst into SEP SRAM (hardware / specification)
|`sep_sram_burst_type_test` is red. A FIXED or WRAP burst into SEP SRAM answers OKAY and runs with INCR beat addresses, on write and on read. The checker accepts either an error response with SRAM unchanged or AXI4 burst semantics (`hw/sys/sep/doc/fabric.adoc`: AXI4 fabric). No SEP document requires refusal; which of the two outcomes the design owes is the open question. Do not XFAIL.

|Dead-space refusal flavour (specification)
|`memory_map.adoc` says the fabric refuses an address past the extent a unit allocates, and names no response flavour for it. `spi_controller` refuses a dead-space write with SLVERR. The access is refused either way, which is the contract `sep_fabric_deadspace_decode_test` asserts, so the flavour is reported as `DEADSPACE-FLAVOUR` for the design owner and is not failed. Which error responses are permitted is the open question.

|TRNG unowned-offset response flavour (specification)
|`hw/sys/sep/doc/memory_map.adoc` requires SLVERR for a TRNG-window offset that owns no register. `doc/integrator/src/smu.adoc` requires a DECERR slave when no external TRNG is connected. The reference integration terminates `ext_trng_axil` with an error slave. `sep_fabric_deadspace_decode_test` `CHK-TRNG-UNOWNED` asserts the refusal both documents require and logs the read and write response codes; which flavour is correct is for the document owner to reconcile. Do not XFAIL.

|ABR unpopulated offsets complete OKAY (vendored collateral)
|`hw/sys/sep/doc/adams_bridge.adoc` `[[abr-aperture-decode]]` documents that the vendored ABR register block has no address check: a word access past the register map (`0xC018`) completes OKAY, a read returns zero and a write is discarded, while a partial-word write answers SLVERR. `sep_abr_access_size_test` `CHK-ABR-UNPOP-RD` / `-WR` / `-PARTIAL` grade that documented behaviour, so an RTL change that refuses these accesses turns them red until the document changes with it.

|KMCSR extent (RDL comment)
|`hw/ip/key_manager/regs/km_csr.rdl` says "Size: 4KB (0x1000)" in its header comment. The generated register block is `KEY_MANAGER_KMCSR_SIZE` = `0x4C0` (`hw/ip/key_manager/regs/gen/c/key_manager_addr.h`). `sep_abr_absent_decerr_test` loads `KMCSR base + 0x4C0` from the KM CPU and logs the answer: AXI_SLVERR with data 0. The 4 KB is the decode window, and the registers end at `0x4C0`. No checker grades this; the header comment is the item to correct.

|Adams Bridge absent build
|Only `sep_abr_absent_decerr_test` runs on the `lsu_stub_abr_absent` model. Every other leaf runs with `SEP_ABR_EN` defined. The ABR memory tie-off and the held-low ABR interrupts of that build have no checker.

|OSS hygiene exclusions
|No licensed/proprietary IP in the build. Bender targets stay `sep` / `sep_el2` / `sep_wrapper` only.

|Verilator covergroups
|SystemVerilog covergroups are not available under Verilator. Feature coverage is the iconic scorecard; VCS Phase 1 bins are in link:SEP_FCOV.adoc[SEP_FCOV]. RTL code coverage is a separate, unthresholded number (`README.md`, Code coverage).

|ROM-FW ownership
|Boot-ROM firmware contracts are out of this VPLAN (separate owner / `rom_fw` class).

|Observation probes
|The TB observation/inject ports are the closed set enumerated in link:SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc[SEP_TB_ARCH].
|Behavioural OTP array
|The array behind the fuse-command interface is a simulation-only behavioural bank, not an OTP macro. Two properties the eFuse programming entries appear to establish are properties of that model rather than of hardware: write-one-to-set additivity is OR-only by construction, so a bank that overwrote instead of OR-ing cannot be represented and no-clobber is not a failable contract; and persistence across cold reset comes from the model having no reset on its storage. What is proven against real logic is that each program reached its addressed bit through the real programming path, and that the sense FSM re-reads the array after reset with the shadow tracking it.

|JTAG MMR token-block access
|`hw/sys/sep/doc/otp_fuse_controller.adoc` allows the MMR token block and denies the shadow, the program/read interface, and the SHIM. No checker claims DECERR on the MMR token block. Inbound ownership under correct programming (CFG, outbound CFG, remap banks, and region CSRs outside every allow window) is owned by `sep_fabric_inbound_filter_rule_matrix_test` `CHK-OWNERSHIP`.
|RTL assertions are backend-dependent
|What participates depends on the backend. Under Verilator, `prim_assert.sv`, `abr_prim_assert.sv` and `ocah_assert.svh` all gate their macros on `VERILATOR`, so design and vendored-IP SVA expand to nothing, and the SEP Verilator targets pass no `--assert`, so the two-state `ocah_axi_sva` rules are dropped as well. Under VCS those macros are live. The shared native profile also defines `COMMON_CELLS_ASSERTS_OFF`, so the PULP `common_cells` inline assertions are off on every backend. Where a checker proves a property, that checker is the whole of the proof on Verilator; no assertion layer sits underneath it there. `sep_km_ic_reset_release_test` is the exception by design: its contract is the `prim_rom` `noXOnCsI` assertion, so its testlist entry runs on VCS only, and a Verilator regression drops it.

|Entropy-pool interrupt numbering (spec)
|`hw/sys/sep/doc/crypto.adoc` numbers the two entropy-pool interrupts one lower than the canonical table in `hw/sys/sep/doc/interrupts.adoc`. PIC source IDs are 1-based, so aggregation index 36/37 is PIC source 37/38; firmware following `crypto.adoc` would enable and mask the wrong two sources. No checker asserts this; do not XFAIL it.

|CPU reset-vector TDR (spec)
|`hw/sys/sep/doc/cpu.adoc` documents a CPU reset-vector TDR selected by a JTAG opcode. The vendored tap implements no such register — the vector reaches the CPU through a direct wrapper input. The testbench shifts that opcode on every CPU-boot run and the sequence is inert; it is not evidence of the TDR.

|Scratch bank register count (spec vs RTL)
|`hw/sys/sep/doc/cpu.adoc` requires "two sets of 16 x 32-bit scratch register banks". `hw/sys/sep/regs/blocks/sep_scratch/sep_scratch.rdl` declares `SCRATCH[8]`, so each bank implements eight registers, not sixteen. The reset-domain behaviour of the banks that exist is proven; the count is not, and no test asserts it. This row is the record of the difference, not a claimed contract. Do not XFAIL.

|AP/STEE output-remap attributes and pass-through (spec)
|`hw/sys/sep/doc/fabric.adoc` gives the AP/STEE remappers a cacheable attribute and says unprogrammed regions are transparent pass-throughs. `hw/ip/output_remap/rtl/output_remap.sv` has neither a valid bit nor a cacheable bit and rewrites the address unconditionally, so an unprogrammed region zeroes the upper address bits instead of passing through. The vehicle on this bank is `sep_fabric_output_remap_datapath_proxy_test`, which proves a programmed window translates and that a beat outside it is refused; the pass-through behaviour of an unprogrammed region has no checker, so this row is the record of the gap and not a claimed contract. Do not XFAIL.

This row is about the AP/STEE bank only. The local-master aliaser is a different block: `hw/ip/axi_alias_remap/rtl/axi_alias_remap.sv` carries `region_valid` per region, drives the outbound cache bits from the region on a hit, and passes an address through unchanged when no region hits. `sep_fabric_local_alias_datapath_test` covers the enable with `CHK-VALID-GATE`.

|Class 2 scan-root count (spec)
|`hw/sys/sep/doc/attack_countermeasures.adoc` states "exactly 10" Class 2 scan roots. The RTL instantiates an eleventh on the entropy-pool EDN leg that is absent from the list, and nothing lints the manifest against the RTL. That manifest is the sole input deciding what is never scan-dumpable.

|`sec_dis` is not forced
|No entry forces `sec_dis`. The LC→`feat_ctrl` stitch stays an end-to-end path; a forced override is not an acceptable substitute for it.

|Checkers sharing one pass line
|Three firmware tests evaluate several conditions and print a single pass line, so their rows do not fail independently of each other: `sep_spi_ot_dma_rx_test` (3 of 3 checkers), `sep_dma_cpu_contention_test` (5 of 5), `sep_mailbox_plic_test` (4 of 4). Firmware counters still gate the verdict.

|Lifecycle codes with no owner
|Closed for the encodings, narrowed for the mechanism. `sep_efuse_lcc_lc_state_stitch_test` walks four of the seven named codes on the OTP-program path (`LC_TEST_DEV`, `LC_PROD`, `LC_RMA_SIP_1`, `LC_RMA_CHIP_1`); the `LC_STATE` write-1-to-set leaves add `LC_RMA_SIP_0`, `LC_RMA_CHIP_0` and `LC_PROD_END` as sensed starting states with a `FEAT_CTRL` claim on each, plus two codes outside the named set. What remains unowned is the *pairing*: `LC_RMA_SIP_0`, `LC_RMA_CHIP_0` and `LC_PROD_END` are sensed but never reached by programming a fuse bit, because the OTP image is a t=0 deposit and a fuse survives reset — from a given image only a W1S superset is reachable in one leaf.

|Contracts not claimed (no vehicle in `all`)
|Phase 3: entropy-pool drain-under-fill; isolate drain-before-reset on the AES and OTBN domains (HMAC, KMAC, Adams Bridge and the shared TRNG drain are proven); locked-field CPU PIC/ISR; ESRC health-test quality and `prim_count` redundancy fault inject; inbound-filter negative product; eleven-output `dbg_disable` gate test; named negative matrices (`sep_cpu_lsu_*_matrix_test`, `sep_fabric_gap_matrix_test`, `sep_irq_extended_connectivity_test`, `sep_efuse_uvm_permission_matrix_test`); KMAC sideload fail-closed + recovery (`ErrKeyNotValid` / `CMD.err_processed`).

|===
